### 0.1 — Middleware disposition (D4)

| Middleware | Verdict | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `BlockPrematureAskMiddleware` | **DELETE** | Regex-blocks `ask_user` whenever the prompt names a framework (`block_premature_ask.py:20-23`) — directly blocks requirement #4, dynamic clarifying questions |
| `BlockTaskToolMiddleware` | **DELETE** | Blocks the `task` tool — blocks requirement #3, subagents for coder/tester/reviewer |
| `ContinueAfterWriteMiddleware` | **DELETE** | Already dead (zero call sites). The new agentic loop owns continuation |
| `EnforceTargetFileMiddleware` | **DELETE** | Only meaningful inside the one-file-per-agent loop being replaced. Also has a basename-matching hole (A1.6) |
| `TaskAnchorMiddleware` | **KEEP, opt-in** | Re-injects the task into the system prompt every model call. Still useful on long 32B runs. Gate behind `[compat] task_anchor = false` |
| `FixWriteParamsMiddleware` | **KEEP, split** | Markdown-fence stripping becomes **required** (0.7.4's `FilesystemBackend.write` no longer strips — Rudra's `OverwriteFilesystemBackend` was doing it). Keep fence-strip + `filename`/`path`→`file_path` aliasing always on; move sandbox-prefix stripping behind `[compat] sandbox_paths = false` |

#### Accepted risk: D4 was not purely subtractive (recorded 2026-08-06)

Deleting `EnforceTargetFileMiddleware` **and** `BlockTaskToolMiddleware` in the same change moved two guardrails at once, in opposite directions:

- The coder **gained a reachable `task` tool** — 0.7.4 registers `task` unconditionally in the default stack (U.16), and nothing blocks it any more.
- The coder **lost path enforcement** — nothing now checks that the file it writes is the file it was assigned.

Meanwhile the orchestrator's success test is unchanged: *does the file exist on disk* (A1.8). So a coder that writes the wrong filename now burns all three attempts, is reported as failed, and **leaves the wrong file behind** — a state that neither middleware permitted before.

**Accepted** per D4 and D6: prompt-level steering (`.rudra/current_task.md`, `_CODER_ANCHOR`, the per-file orchestrator message, and the coder prompt's `Do NOT call task.` rule) is expected to suffice at 32B, and `EnforceTargetFileMiddleware`'s own basename hole (A1.6) meant it never actually closed this. Do **not** restore either middleware. **Re-evaluate at C6.2**, when subagents become a designed feature rather than an unblocked side effect — the right replacement is a real completion gate (C6.6), not a path matcher.

**Re-evaluated 2026-08-12 at C6.2 (Step 9b), as this note directed. Both halves are now answered, and the answer is the one predicted:**

- **The completion gate exists.** `C6.6` shipped in Step 9a as `src/rudra/verify/`, and its stub scan plus syntax stage catch the "wrong or empty file left behind" state that neither deleted middleware actually prevented. A path matcher was never the right tool, and none was added.
- **The `task` exposure is now designed rather than incidental, and it narrowed.** Step 9b measured that deepagents auto-adds a `general-purpose` subagent carrying the main agent's whole tool list and **no deny middleware**, unless a spec of that exact name suppresses it (`graph.py:751`). So "nothing blocks `task` any more" was worse than this note knew: reaching it handed the model an ungated agent. Rudra now ships its own `general-purpose` — read-only, gated, `default` model role — which takes that slot. Every Rudra subagent is assembled by one function that always injects the gate, and `RudraSubagent` has no `middleware` field for a caller to forget.

`task` remains reachable from any agent given subagents. That is now a known, bounded surface with the deny layer inside it, rather than an unexamined one. The remaining half — a coder writing a file it was not assigned — is `A1.8`'s, and dies with the loop in Step 9c.

### 0.2 — Fix-loop design (D9)

**Decision: hand-rolled deterministic loop. `RubricMiddleware` is deferred, not adopted.**

Primary reason is not reliability — it is **role overlap**. C6.3 already plans a reviewer subagent, i.e. the LLM-judgment layer. `RubricMiddleware` would add a *second* LLM judge that additionally **controls whether the agent is allowed to stop** (`rubric.py:92` — only `satisfied` / `failed` / `max_iterations` terminate). Two judges, one holding the exit door.

Clean split instead:

| Layer | Decides | Authority |
|---|---|---|
| Deterministic gate (C6.6) | done / not done | **Blocking.** Test exit code is ground truth |
| Reviewer subagent (C6.3) | quality | **Advisory.** Never gates the loop |

Secondary reasons, all sharper at 32B (D6):
- No extra inference pass per stop-attempt — matters for local latency and memory
- No structured-output fragility. Upstream already guards against self-contradictory grader responses (`rubric.py:293-297`), which implies that failure is common enough to need a guard
- The grader reads the **whole transcript** — a real context-window problem on a 32B model mid-session
- Failures are debuggable: a pytest traceback, not a grader's prose

**Cost of this choice, and how it is paid:** a test-only gate misses stubs, `TODO`s, unhandled errors. Covered deterministically in C6.6 — no judge needed.

**Revisit trigger:** if C6.6's deterministic gate proves too weak in practice, re-open `RubricMiddleware` as a *final completeness check only*, with a tight `max_iterations` — never as the loop controller.

### 0.3 — Superpowers skill selection (D10 + D11)

Verified against the upstream tree: **14 skills, 3,185 lines of `SKILL.md`, plus 36 supporting files** (`references/*.md`, helper scripts, prompt templates).

#### Vendor everything; gate enablement separately

Owner's rule is "don't strip anything that can help Rudra." Chasing the reference graph proves that rule converges on the whole set: adding `executing-plans` immediately pulled in three more skills (below). So the two questions get separated:

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What gets **vendored** (on disk, resolvable, `read_file`-able) | **All 14.** Cost is disk, not context. Eliminates dangling references entirely |
| What gets **enabled** (name+description in the prompt index) | **8 by default**, rest opt-in via `[skills] enabled = [...]` in TOML |

`SkillsMiddleware` uses progressive disclosure (`middleware/skills.py:3`, `:738`, `:757`) — only `name` + `description` enter the prompt; the body is fetched with `read_file` on demand. Enablement is therefore a prompt-index decision, not a disk decision.

**Enabled by default (8) + bootstrap:**

| Skill | Lines | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `brainstorming` | 151 | Requirement #1 — plan before coding, dynamic clarification |
| `writing-plans` | 168 | Requirement #1 — plan artifact |
| `executing-plans` | 64 | Requirement #1 — a plan Rudra writes but cannot execute is half a workflow |
| `systematic-debugging` | 283 | Requirement #4 — fix wrong code |
| `test-driven-development` | 320 | Requirement #4 — testing; `systematic-debugging` leans on it |
| `verification-before-completion` | 120 | Requirement #4 — pairs with the C6.6 deterministic gate |
| `requesting-code-review` | 95 | Requirement #4 — main agent → reviewer subagent (C6.3) |
| `receiving-code-review` | 205 | Requirement #4 — acting on reviewer feedback, i.e. the C6.5 fix loop |
| `using-superpowers` | 62 | **Bootstrap, not optional.** Tracked as C5.3 |

> **Owner said "code-review" — no skill has that name.** Upstream splits it into `requesting-` and `receiving-`. Both halves map onto Rudra's reviewer-subagent design, so both ship.

**Vendored but off by default (5)** — flip on once their prerequisite lands:

|---|---|---|
| `using-git-worktrees` | 167 | C3.5 (git tools) lands. Genuinely valuable — isolates agent work from the user's working tree |
| `finishing-a-development-branch` | 201 | C3.5 lands. Pairs with worktrees |

### 0.4 — Skill delivery mechanism (D13)

#### The constraint the README exposes

Per `README.md:99-164`, `rudra` is installed **once, globally** (pipx or venv; C10.5 adds PyPI). The user then `cd`s into **many different project directories** and runs `rudra`.

But deepagents' `FilesystemBackend` is rooted at the **project path** (`main_agent.py`, the `FilesystemBackend(root_dir=str(project_path), virtual_mode=True)` construction in `create_main_agent`), and skill sources are **backend-relative paths** (`skills.py`, "Path Conventions"). Vendored skills live in `site-packages` — **outside the backend root, therefore unreadable**.

This kills the naive "just point `skills=` at the package directory" approach, and it is why Option 1 (two directories inside the package) was rejected: `site-packages` is read-only on a pipx/PyPI install.

#### The mechanism

```python
CompositeBackend(
    default = LocalShellBackend(root_dir=project_path),           # project files + execute
    routes  = {"/skills/": FilesystemBackend(root_dir=cache_dir)},
)
create_deep_agent(skills=["/skills/active/"], backend=composite, ...)
```

Verified in the 0.7.4 wheel:
- `filesystem.py:155-159` — `virtual_mode=True`'s stated primary use case is exactly this: `CompositeBackend` strips the route prefix and forwards normalized paths
- `composite.py:774` — `CompositeBackend.execute` delegates to `default` when it is a `SandboxBackendProtocol`, so shell still works rooted at the project (C3.1 unaffected)

#### Cache layout

```
${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/rudra/skills/<key>/
  library/    all 14, rewritten. Readable via read_file, NOT indexed.
  active/     copies of the 8 enabled. Passed as the skills source.
```

`<key>` = hash of (rudra version + upstream skills SHA + transform version + sorted enabled list). Built on first run; reused by every project sharing that config. A project whose TOML enables a different set gets its own key.

Two properties fall out, and both matter:

| Property | Consequence |
|---|---|
| **Enablement = which directory is a source** | No allowlist parameter needed. **G6 answered with zero deepagents patching** — avoids a second `compat/` monkeypatch on top of the U.4 hazard |
| **Cross-refs always point at `/skills/library/<name>/`** | One canonical path regardless of enablement. The C5.4 rename transform never needs to know what is enabled, and enabling a skill later (C5.9) rewrites no references |

#### Why the cache, not `.rudra/` and not the package

| Location | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `~/.cache/rudra/skills/` | **Chosen.** One install, many project dirs → shared. Nothing written to the install dir, so read-only `site-packages` is fine |
| `<project>/.rudra/skills/` | Rejected — copies ~3,185 lines + 36 support files into **every** project directory. Contradicts D7's memory/footprint goal |
| Inside the package | Rejected — read-only under pipx/PyPI installs |

### 0.5 — Static Q&A purge (C6.8a)

Requirement #1: *"if anything is not cleared from prompt it should ask user questions. this should be dynamic. no static QA."*

The current static questionnaire is **not one file**. It is a fixed 4-field model — language / framework / database / notes — threaded through 7 places. Deleting `ProjectContext` alone leaves 6 that silently re-impose the same 4 boxes.

#### Inventory — all verified

| # | Surface | Evidence | Failure it causes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ProjectContext` — 4 fixed fields | `state/project_config.py:11-17` | The data model *is* the questionnaire. Nothing else can be recorded |
| 2 | `save_project_context` hardcoded allowlist | `interaction_tools.py:67` | Any fact outside the 4 fields is **silently discarded** — no error, no warning |
| 3 | `ask_user` docstring steers to language/framework/database **and mandates "ONE focused question at a time"** | `interaction_tools.py:32` | Caps the question space, and **directly contradicts C6.8's batching requirement** |
| 4 | `_ensure_agents_md` renders the same 4 fields | `main_agent.py:454-461` | Project memory can only remember those 4 things |
| 5 | `_write_tech_stack_file` renders the same 4, plus a **hardcoded 8-row inference table** (Flask/Django/FastAPI/React/Next.js/Express/Spring/Rails) | `main_agent.py:484-506` | **Static inference, the twin of static Q&A.** "Build a Rust CLI with clap" matches nothing and falls through to "infer the most likely one and proceed" |
| 6 | Planner prompt: *"Do NOT call ask_user() if the task already specifies a framework or language"* | `planner_agent.py:63` (verified 2026-08-06 — the last line of `build_planner_prompt`'s `## RULES` block) | Prompt-level twin of `BlockPrematureAskMiddleware`. **Deleting the middleware in C0.2 does not remove this** |
| ~~7~~ | ~~`README.md:626-636` documents the literal 4-question form~~ — **ALREADY GONE, citation stale (A1.69).** Measured 2026-08-13: `README.md` is 261 lines and mentions none of the four fields. An earlier step rewrote it and this row was never revisited. **6 live surfaces, not 7** | — | — |

Plus the on-disk `.rudra/project.json` schema, which mirrors #1.

#### Target design

| Concern | Replace with |
|---|---|
| Storage (#1, #4, #7) | **Open key/value fact store.** Agent records whatever it actually established — `{"language": "Rust", "cli_framework": "clap", "min_rust_version": "1.75", "no_async": true}`. Keys are not enumerated anywhere in code |
| Tool (#2) | Drop `valid_fields`. Accept arbitrary keys; validate types and size, not names |
| Tool contract (#3) | Rewrite `ask_user` to accept **a batch of related questions** and return a mapping. Delete "ONE question at a time" — it is the opposite of what C6.8 needs |
| Generated context (#5) | Render `tech_stack.md` from whatever facts exist, in insertion order. **Delete the 8-row inference table entirely** — inferring the stack is the model's job, not a lookup table's |
| Prompt rules (#6) | Replace the prohibition with a budget: *ask only what you cannot infer from the prompt or the codebase; batch related questions; you have N questions* |
| Docs (#7) | Rewrite alongside A4.1 |

**Migration:** existing `.rudra/project.json` files carry the 4 old keys. Read them as ordinary facts — the old field names become unremarkable keys in the open store, so no migration script is needed.

**Acceptance test for this item:** `rudra "build a CLI in Rust with clap"` in an empty directory must record `rust` and `clap` as facts and generate a Rust plan. Today surface #5 has no Rust row and surface #6 forbids asking, so the run is steered toward a Python/JS default.

### 0.6 — MemPalace storage scope (D14)

Verified by unpacking the `mempalace` 3.6.0 wheel.

#### Project-scoped palace is natively supported

| Mechanism | Evidence |
|---|---|
| `Config(palace_path=...)` constructor override | `config.py:368`, `:385` — clean Python API, matches D3 |
| `MEMPALACE_PALACE_PATH` env var | `config.py:387` |
| Default when unset | `~/.mempalace/palace` (`config.py:221`) — **user-global, must be overridden** |
| `tunnel_file` / `hallway_file` are siblings of `palace_path` | `config.py:397-410`. Docstring states this was changed *specifically* so "multiple palaces on one host" stop sharing one file → multi-palace is deliberate design |

Layout:

```
<project>/.rudra/memory/
  palace/         ChromaDB store  (palace_path)
  tunnels.json    sibling, automatic
  hallways.json   sibling, automatic
```

#### State that stays user-global regardless of palace_path

| Path | Evidence | Handling |
|---|---|---|
| `~/.mempalace/config.json` | `config.py:4`, `:364`, `:393` | **Must be explicitly overridden.** A user's personal MemPalace config would otherwise silently change Rudra's embedding model / backend — a bug that reproduces on one machine only |
| `~/.mempalace/entity_registry.json` | `entity_registry.py:299` | Accept, or point elsewhere if the API allows. Investigate in C8.1 |
| `~/.mempalace/hook_state/`, `state/` | `hooks_cli.py:23-24`, `diary_ingest.py:50` | Not used by Rudra's integration path — verify they stay dormant |
| HuggingFace embedding-model cache | `embedding.py:7-20` | **Leave global — this is the desirable case.** D12's pre-fetch downloads once and every project reuses it |

#### Governing rule this settles

| Nature of the data | Location |
|---|---|
| Identical across projects | **User cache** — skills (D13), embedding model |
| Unique per project | **Project dir** — palace, checkpoints, config, plan |

#### Durability

The palace is binary and churns on every write, so it must never be the only copy of anything worth keeping. `export_palace(palace_path, output_dir, format="markdown")` (`exporter.py:68`) writes durable markdown into `.rudra/memory/export/`, which D15 places in the committable subtree. Whether the user commits it is their choice; Rudra's job is to make that choice safe. See §0.7.

### 0.7 — `.rudra/` directory layout (D15)

#### Correcting a wrong premise

An earlier revision of this file claimed "`.rudra/` is gitignored". **That is false as a general statement.** `.gitignore:213` in *this* repo ignores `.rudra/` only because `rudra` was run here during development. Verified:

- `.rudra/` is created automatically by `main_agent.py:559-560`
- **No code anywhere writes to the project's `.gitignore`** (`grep -rn "gitignore" --include=*.py src/` → only *read* paths, in `virtual_fs.py`)
- `README.md:592-596` merely *suggests* the user add it

Whether `.rudra/` is committed is the **user's decision**, and Rudra must not make it for them.

#### The actual defect

`.rudra/` currently mixes durable text and volatile binary in one flat directory, so both user choices are bad:

| Choice | Outcome today |
|---|---|
| Commit `.rudra/` | Commits `checkpoints.db` — **61 KB in this repo, 278 KB in `coding-files/` after one small task** — plus, once C8 lands, a ChromaDB store that rewrites on every memory write |
| Gitignore `.rudra/` | Loses `AGENTS.md`, clarified facts, and the memory export — the durable context that is the entire point of C7.3 and Phase 8 |

#### Target layout

```
.rudra/
  .gitignore      written by Rudra, scoping ONLY its own directory
  config.toml     durable
  AGENTS.md       durable   (C7.3 living document)
  facts.json      durable   (open fact store, C6.8a — replaces project.json)
  memory/
    export/       durable   markdown export (exporter.py:68)
    palace/       volatile  ChromaDB                    [ignored]
  run/
    checkpoints.db  volatile                            [ignored]
    PLAN.md         volatile  current-run state         [ignored]
    logs/           volatile                            [ignored]
```

Committing `.rudra/` becomes safe by default; ignoring it becomes a deliberate, informed loss. Rudra writes only `.rudra/.gitignore`, which affects nothing outside its own directory.

### 0.8 — Real MCP acceptance target (D19)

**Owner requirement, recorded 2026-08-10.** Phase 4 (`C4.1`–`C4.6`, Step 13) must be proven against a **real, external MCP server**, not a fixture and not a scripted model. The named server is **`https://github.com/archish9/VersionControlHelperMCP`**.

This section exists so Step 13's implementation plan inherits the requirement instead of rediscovering it. **Nothing here is to be built before Step 13** — the owner explicitly declined to pull MCP work forward into Step 6.

#### The three constraints, in the owner's framing

| Constraint | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Real test, not a simulation** | An actual `uv run version-control-helper-mcp` subprocess over stdio, connected to by Rudra, driven by a real model, actually invoking the server's git tools. The `ScriptedToolModel` pattern (`tests/test_deepagents_contract.py`, U.19) is **not** acceptable as the acceptance evidence — it may still be used for the cheap always-on CI tests, but it does not close this item |
| **Never ships** | Zero entries in `pyproject.toml`. Not vendored into `src/rudra/`. Not a default MCP server (this constrains **C4.6**'s shipped bundle — this server is not a candidate for it). Rudra must work identically with it absent |
| **Documentation only** | A worked example in `Documentation/`: what MCP is in Rudra, how to clone and launch this server, the exact `.mcp.json`, and what a real run looks like. Presented as a **suggestion and demo**, clearly marked as a separate project the user installs themselves |

#### What the server is — verified 2026-08-10

Read from the GitHub README on 2026-08-10. **Nobody has cloned, installed, or executed it yet** — every line below is a documentation claim, not a measured one, and Step 13's first task should be to verify it locally before designing against it.

| Property | Claim |
|---|---|
| Runtime | Python 3.11+, `uv` |
| Install | `git clone …` then `uv sync` |
| Launch | `uv run version-control-helper-mcp`; repo target via `REPO_PATH=/path/to/project uv run version-control-helper-mcp` |
| Transport | **stdio** — matches `C4.1`'s stdio path, so the HTTP/SSE path stays unproven by this test and needs its own evidence |
| Tools (10) | `initialize_repo`, `get_repo_status`, `commit_all_changes`, `list_commits`, `rollback_to_commit`, `compare_commits`, `create_branch`, `switch_branch`, `list_branches`, `generate_commit_message` |
| License | **Unverified.** Does not gate anything (nothing is vendored), but the doc page must state it correctly — check before writing `C4.8` |

Same author as Rudra (`archish9`), so "third-party" is the wrong word in user-facing text; it is a **separate project by the same author**, and the documentation should say so plainly rather than implying a bundled or supported component.

#### Safety constraint — binding, not advisory

Two of the ten tools destroy work: `rollback_to_commit` (git reset) and `commit_all_changes` (stages and commits everything, including files the user did not intend to commit). Handing these to an LLM-driven agent means the agent can discard uncommitted changes.

**The acceptance run must target a throwaway repository created by `mktemp -d`** — never the Rudra checkout, never any repo the owner cares about. `REPO_PATH` must be set explicitly to that tempdir; relying on the server's default (whatever it turns out to be) is not acceptable. This also has to be stated prominently on the documentation page, since a user following the guide will point it at a real project.

This is a concrete instance of what Step 7's permission layer (`C3.3`, `U.7`) exists for, and a good forcing function for it: MCP tools are *not* covered by `permissions=`, which governs filesystem tools only. **Open design question for Step 13: does an MCP tool call pass through Rudra's permission gate at all, or does it bypass it entirely?** Today the honest answer is that no gate exists. Whatever `C4.x` builds must answer this, and `rollback_to_commit` is the test case that makes the answer matter.

#### Test design the Step 13 plan should adopt

Two runs, in this order — the isolation run first so a failure in the combined run is attributable:

1. **MCP-only run.** Task is purely git: initialize a repo, create a branch, commit, list commits, compare two commits. No file-writing by Rudra's own loop. A failure here is unambiguously an MCP failure — connection, schema translation, or invocation
2. **Combined run.** Rudra writes a small program using its existing capability, then commits it through the MCP server. This is the realistic integration proof, and the one the documentation page walks through

Evidence to capture for both, in the ledger row itself rather than in an ephemeral scratch file (the U.12 lesson — its `/tmp` smoke logs no longer resolve): the exact command, the `.mcp.json` used, which tools the model actually called, the resulting `git log` from the tempdir, and the exit code.

Additional assertions worth building into the same run rather than deferring:

- **`C4.4` lazy loading actually holds.** Ten tools from one server is a real payload; assert the schemas are not all in the prompt before invocation. A 32B context is the constraint this is protecting
- **`C4.5` namespacing.** This server's `create_branch` / `list_branches` are exactly the kind of names that will collide with Rudra's own git tools from `C3.5` (Step 8). Verify `server__tool` disambiguation with both present
- **Absence is graceful.** With the server not running, or `.mcp.json` naming a command that does not exist, Rudra must degrade with a clear message and still complete a normal non-MCP task — not crash. This mirrors `C8.6`'s rule for MemPalace

#### Known risk this test will hit

**A1.39** — a transient provider error anywhere in a run kills the whole run and reports failure even when the work succeeded. The acceptance run needs a live hosted model, which is exactly the condition under which A1.39 was observed (twice, on OpenRouter's free tier). If A1.39 is still open when Step 13 runs, expect retries and do not misread a provider 502 as an MCP failure.

---

It has **no ledger entry anywhere** prior to this row and is a live data-loss path today, independent of U.3/U.14/U.15. Root cause: the function cannot distinguish "the model wrapped real code in a fence because that's the write_file convention it learned" from "the fenced block genuinely is the entire file content" — heuristically these look identical. Not fixed here — out of this fix wave's scope; would need either a smarter heuristic (e.g. only strip when content-after-unwrap still looks like the target file's expected language) or an opt-out per target filename/extension (e.g. skip stripping for `.md` targets) | `src/rudra/middleware/fix_write_params.py:34-36` |

Verified: `ChatOllama.profile is None` → Rudra takes the fallback. A 32B model (D6) has 32k–128k context, so it **overflows long before 170k**. The only real context-management mechanism in the product is inert for its primary target. Fix: set `trigger`/`keep` explicitly per role from configured context size, or register a model profile. | `.venv/.../deepagents/middleware/summarization.py:249-286`; `python -c "ChatOllama(...).profile"` → `None` |
| A1.19 | **DONE** 2026-08-06 | **`project_tree()` returns `(empty project)` for a directory that has files.** Found by final whole-branch review 2026-08-06. `_git_listing` (`src/rudra/filesystem/tree.py`, `_git_listing`) returns `None` only on *failure* — git missing, exit ≠ 0, timeout. But when the root is inside a git work tree and is itself **gitignored**, `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` exits **0 with empty stdout**, so `_git_listing` returns `[]`, which is not `None`, and `project_tree`'s `if entries is None:` branch never reaches the `_walk_listing` fallback. Result: a directory full of files reports `(empty project)` into the planner prompt (`src/rudra/agent/planner_agent.py`, `build_planner_prompt`'s `## PROJECT STRUCTURE` section). **Reproduced 2026-08-06:** a repo whose `.gitignore` contains `playground/`, with `playground/main.py` and `playground/src/mod.py` on disk — `git -C <repo>/playground ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard -z` → `rc=0`, `stdout=b''`; `project_tree(<repo>/playground)` → `'(empty project)'`. **Regression against `VirtualFileSystem.get_tree()`**, which walked disk and had no git path at all. **Fixed 2026-08-06:** the fallback now triggers on a falsy listing, not only on `None`. Deliberate consequence, recorded in the `project_tree` docstring: in a directory where everything is ignored, the walk lists ignored files — the correct answer for "show the agent what is here", and what the pre-branch VFS did | `src/rudra/filesystem/tree.py` (`project_tree`, `_git_listing`); test `tests/test_project_tree.py::test_gitignored_root_still_lists_its_own_files` |
| A1.20 | PENDING (partial fix applied 2026-08-06) | **`_stream_coder`'s repeated-tool-call guard does not watch `task`.** The guard counts repeats only for `write_file` / `edit_file` / `read_file` (`src/rudra/agent/main_agent.py`, `_stream_coder`, the guarded-name tuple), so a coder looping on `task` is unbounded. `task` became reachable when `BlockTaskToolMiddleware` was deleted (D4 / C0.2) — deepagents 0.7.4 registers `task` unconditionally in the default tool stack (U.16 probe: `['delete', 'edit_file', 'execute', 'glob', 'grep', 'ls', 'read_file', 'task', 'write_file']`). **Partially fixed 2026-08-06:** `"task"` added to the guarded-name tuple. **A second, deeper issue is NOT fixed, and this row stays PENDING for it:** `_stream_coder` streams with `subgraphs=True` but keeps a **single `processed` counter across all namespaces**. Parent and subagent each carry their own `messages` list; once a subagent's list outgrows the parent's, `processed` has already advanced past the parent's length, so the parent's later messages never enter the `while processed < len(msgs)` body and the guard silently stops seeing them. `_stream_planner` has the identical structure and the identical hole. Fixing it properly means per-namespace state, not one counter — belongs with C6.2 (subagents) / C9.1 (trace rework). **DONE 2026-08-12 (Step 9b), on the half C6.2 owns.** The guard moved out of `_stream_coder` into `src/rudra/subagents/runner.py`, where `_WATCHED_TOOLS` includes `task` and `_call_key` keys a `task` call by its `subagent_type` — so three delegations to the same subagent halt, which is the case the old guard named but could never reach, because the coder had `tools=[]` and no `task` tool existed to call. With a `general-purpose` subagent registered, one now does. Covered by `tests/test_subagents_runner.py::test_the_task_tool_is_watched_by_the_repeat_guard`. **The `processed`-counter half stays open and moves to C9.1**: `runner.py` carries the same single-counter-across-namespaces shape, because it inherits the `subgraphs=True` chunk contract deliberately (changing `stream_mode` would change the chunk shape every parse loop depends on). That is a trace-rework problem, not a subagent one | `src/rudra/agent/main_agent.py` (`_stream_coder`: the guarded-name tuple, the `processed` counter, the `subgraphs=True` `astream` call); same shape in `_stream_planner`; now also `src/rudra/subagents/runner.py` |
| A1.21 | PENDING | **`project_tree`'s non-git fallback diverges from git in three ways, none of which the tests cover.** Found by final whole-branch review 2026-08-06. Moot whenever git answers — it bites only in a non-repo directory, or (post-A1.19) in a gitignored root. (1) **Directory-only semantics discarded:** `_to_wcmatch` (`src/rudra/filesystem/tree.py`) does `line.rstrip("/")`, so a `.gitignore` line `build/` — which git applies to *directories only* — also excludes a plain **file** named `build`. (2) **Only the root `.gitignore` is read:** `_gitignore_patterns` opens `root / ".gitignore"` and nothing else — nested `.gitignore` files in subdirectories, `.git/info/exclude`, and `core.excludesFile` are all ignored, so the fallback under-excludes wherever a project splits its ignore rules. (3) **The tests exercise exactly one pattern.** `tests/test_project_tree.py` uses `build/` in every gitignore-related test; `!` negation, leading-`/` anchoring and `**` are asserted **nowhere**, even though `_to_wcmatch`'s docstring claims all three and A1.10 was closed on that claim. Fix alongside any future work that makes the fallback load-bearing | `src/rudra/filesystem/tree.py` (`_to_wcmatch`, `_gitignore_patterns`); `tests/test_project_tree.py` |

A user copying the example silently gets different sampling behavior from the documented default. Fix with **A1.5** | `.env.example:11` vs `src/rudra/config.py:20`. **Verified 2026-08-07 (Step 3, Task 4):** `.env.example`'s `OLLAMA_TEMPERATURE` now reads `0.3`, matching `config.py`'s coded default |

An agent would plan to create files that already exist on disk. **Fixed 2026-08-07:** the set is split into `_ROOT_ANCHORED_SKIP_DIRS` (the five generic names above, matched only as the first path segment) and a narrowed `_ALWAYS_SKIP_DIRS` (the twelve unambiguous tool/VCS names — `node_modules`, `__pycache__`, `.next`, `.angular`, `.venv`, `venv`, `.tox`, `.pytest_cache`, `.ruff_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, `.git`, `.rudra` — matched at any depth). Both `_is_always_skipped` (the git-listing path) and `_walk_listing`'s dirname-pruning (the fallback path) updated identically, gated on `is_root = rel_dir == Path(".")`. New tests: `test_nested_generic_named_dirs_are_not_hidden` (`src/out/writer.py` IS listed) and `test_root_level_generic_build_dirs_are_still_hidden` (root-level `out/bundle.js` is NOT listed); the existing Task 4 root-level tests (`test_build_output_dirs_are_never_listed`, `test_frontend_build_dirs_are_never_listed`) pass unmodified. `test_skip_dirs_are_sourced_from_the_stack_registry`'s invariant updated to `ALL_SKIP_DIRS <= (_ALWAYS_SKIP_DIRS | _ROOT_ANCHORED_SKIP_DIRS)`, since neither set alone is a superset of the registry any more by design. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_project_tree.py -v` → 14 passed; `.venv/bin/pytest -q` → 116 passed (114 baseline + 2 new); `.venv/bin/ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; `.venv/bin/ruff format --check src/ tests/` → clean | `src/rudra/filesystem/tree.py` (`_ROOT_ANCHORED_SKIP_DIRS`, `_ALWAYS_SKIP_DIRS`, `_is_always_skipped`, `_walk_listing`); `tests/test_project_tree.py` |

| A1.31 | PENDING | **`resolve_test_command` conflates two different "no answer" cases.** Found by final whole-branch review 2026-08-07. `src/rudra/stacks/detect.py`'s `resolve_test_command` (`:68-85`) returns `None` for a Node project (no `profile.test_command`) in two distinct situations: (1) `package.json` is present, valid JSON, and genuinely declares no `scripts.test` — a real answer; and (2) `package.json` is missing, or exists but fails to parse — `_load_package_json` (`:17-29`) degrades both to `{}` inside a bare `except (OSError, ValueError): return {}`, indistinguishable from case (1) once `.get("scripts")` runs against the result. The function's own docstring says "None is a real answer, not a failure," which is true of producer (1) and false of producer (2). Verified directly, three inputs into `resolve_test_command(tmp_path, NODE)`: no `package.json` on disk → `None`; `package.json` containing `{"scripts": {}}` → `None`; `package.json` containing invalid JSON (`"{not valid json"`) → `None` — all three indistinguishable from the caller's side. This matters because a later fix-loop step (**C6.5**, `write → verify → diagnose → fix → reverify`) needs opposite handling for the two cases: producer (1) means skip the test gate (nothing to run), producer (2) means the manifest itself is broken and the loop should retry/fix it, not silently skip testing. Not fixed here — `src/rudra/stacks/` is out of this fix wave's permitted scope. See also **A1.32**, the concrete real-world case this ambiguity bites hardest | `src/rudra/stacks/detect.py:17-29` (`_load_package_json`), `:68-85` (`resolve_test_command`); verified via direct call, three inputs (absent / valid-no-test / malformed `package.json`) all returning `None`. Cross-ref **C6.5**, **C11.1** |
| A1.32 | PENDING | **`npm init -y`'s default test stub produces a gate that can never pass.** Found by final whole-branch review 2026-08-07. `npm init -y` writes `"test": "echo \"Error: no test specified\" && exit 1"` into `package.json`'s `scripts.test` — a non-empty string, so `resolve_test_command` (`src/rudra/stacks/detect.py:68-85`) returns `["npm", "test"]`, a command that unconditionally exits 1. This is the single most likely real-world Node case Rudra will hit: a greenfield project the agent itself scaffolds with `npm init -y` before installing dependencies. Existing coverage (`tests/test_stacks.py:172-209`) exercises a declared-and-working test command, an empty/absent `scripts.test`, and malformed/list/scalar-shaped `package.json` — never this stub. Verified directly: `resolve_test_command(tmp_path, NODE)` against a `package.json` whose `scripts.test` is set to the exact `npm init -y` string → `['npm', 'test']`. Same root cause and same not-fixed-here scope as **A1.31** — `resolve_test_command` cannot tell "a real, always-failing placeholder command" from "a real test command that happens to be failing right now," which is a second, narrower instance of A1.31's None-conflation problem one level up (this one returns a non-`None` answer that is just as useless as `None` would be). Not fixed here | `src/rudra/stacks/detect.py:68-85`; verified via direct call against a `package.json` containing the literal `npm init -y` stub string; `tests/test_stacks.py:172-209` (existing coverage, does not include this case). Cross-ref **A1.31** |
| A1.33 | PENDING | **Three smaller findings from the same final whole-branch review 2026-08-07, grouped as one row (same class as A2.15).** (a) `detect()` (`src/rudra/stacks/detect.py:50-65`) returns `[]` for both a nonexistent/non-directory path and a genuine greenfield directory with no marker files — identical answers for "there is nothing here to detect" and "this path doesn't exist," though the docstring (`:57`, "An empty list means greenfield -- no marker files yet.") documents only the second. Verified: `detect(Path("/tmp/does_not_exist_xyz"))` → `[]`, same as `detect(Path(<empty tmp dir>))` → `[]`. (b) `registry.py`'s `PYTHON` profile hardcodes `test_command=("pytest",)` (`registry.py:14`) — a bare executable name, not `["python", "-m", "pytest"]` or a venv-relative path, so it is not on `PATH` in a project virtualenv Rudra did not itself activate, and is simply wrong for a Django (`manage.py test`) or stdlib-`unittest` project that never installed `pytest` at all. **(b) FIXED 2026-08-11 in Step 8**, because `C3.6`'s runner consumes exactly this field — see that step's spec §6.1. `PYTHON.test_command` is now `None`, and `resolve_test_command` works the argv out from the target project's own layout, ordered by evidence strength: project venv `pytest`, then the venv interpreter with `-m pytest`, then `manage.py test`, then a declared pytest with `-m pytest`, then `-m unittest discover`. Nothing reads Rudra's own venv (D18). **The measured failure is worse than the row above describes.** On this machine a bare `pytest` *is* on PATH — `/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.12/bin/pytest`, pytest 9.0.3 — so the old code would not have failed loudly; it would have run the **wrong** interpreter. The project's own is `.venv/bin/pytest`, pytest 9.0.2, shebanged to `.venv/bin/python`, and the system interpreter does not even have the project's dependencies installed. So "not on PATH" was the optimistic reading: the realistic one is a suite run under an unrelated environment, reporting import errors as test failures straight into Step 9's fix loop. Verified after the fix: `resolve_test_command(Path('.'), PYTHON)` → `['.venv/bin/pytest']`. Ten regression tests in `tests/test_stacks.py`, including one asserting no subprocess is ever started, since `stacks/` observes and `C3.6` executes. (a) and (c) remain **PENDING**. (c) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has no top-level `permissions:` block — verified: `grep -n "^permissions:" .github/workflows/ci.yml` → no hits across all 55 lines — so its three jobs (`lint`, `test`, `coverage`) run with the default token scope for the repository rather than an explicit least-privilege grant. None of the three fixed here — (a)/(b) are `src/rudra/stacks/` and (c) is `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, both out of this fix wave's permitted scope | (a) `src/rudra/stacks/detect.py:50-65`; (b) `src/rudra/stacks/registry.py:14`; (c) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (55 lines total, no `permissions:` key anywhere). Cross-ref **C11.2**, **C11.3**, **C10.6** |

The generated file was correct and executable (`python wordcount.py sample.txt` → `2 lines 5 words 24 chars`), so the user is told the run failed while the work in fact succeeded. Two runs in a row hit this; an earlier acceptance run needed a second attempt for the same reason. **Not a Step 5 regression** — nothing in the model factory handles or suppresses provider errors, and the pre-Step-5 `ChatOllama` path had no retry either; the failure only became visible now that a hosted, rate-limited backend is reachable. Worth pairing with **A1.35**: the Nemotron 3 Ultra harness profile deepagents ships for this exact model bundles a `ToolRetryMiddleware` (`profiles/harness/_nvidia_nemotron_3_ultra.py`), and A1.35 is precisely the finding that it can never load. Fix belongs with the Step 9 loop rewrite (`C6.1`–`C6.6`) or earlier if it bites: retry with backoff on 429/5xx, and a completion summary that reports files actually written rather than treating any exception as total failure | run log from a `mktemp -d` on `e238576`; `.rudra/PLAN.md` = `- [x] wordcount.py` with `wordcount.py` present and working on disk; `.venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/langchain_openai/chat_models/base.py:1828` (`raise ValueError(response_dict.get("error"))`); `src/rudra/agent/main_agent.py` `RudraAgent.run` has no try/except around agent invocation |

| A1.50 | **DONE** 2026-08-10 | **`floor_disable = ["outside-root"]` cannot do what it says for filesystem tools, and fails silently.** The backend is constructed `virtual_mode=True`, which makes deepagents treat every path as relative to the virtual root and confine it to `root_dir` — so `write_file`/`edit_file`/`delete` can never leave the project regardless of Rudra's floor. The user asked to disable a restriction, Rudra said yes, and the write silently went somewhere else. Two honest resolutions: **(a)** drop `outside-root` from `floor_disable`'s accepted values, since the floor rule is redundant with `virtual_mode` for the tools it covers and un-disableable in practice — the remaining names (`git-dir`, `catastrophic-command`) are genuinely Rudra's; or **(b)** make disabling it also relax the backend, i.e. construct without `virtual_mode`, which reopens the 0.4.12 path-escape U.19 documented and should not be done casually. **(a) is the recommended answer.** Note this cuts the other way too, and in Rudra's favour: filesystem tools are confined by the backend, not by the floor, so the floor's `outside-root` rule is defence in depth rather than the only guard. Found by executing the Step 7 acceptance runs. **FIXED 2026-08-10 with (a).** `floor_disable` now rejects `outside-root` with an error explaining that confinement comes from the backend and that shell is not covered by it either; `git-dir` and `catastrophic-command` stay disableable because those *are* Rudra's. Accepting a name and then not honouring it is the defect — rejecting it is the fix. `DISABLEABLE_FLOOR_RULES` in `config/schema.py`; the floor itself still evaluates all three rules, so nothing is weakened | Step 7 acceptance run, audit log and resulting file path quoted above; `src/rudra/agent/main_agent.py::build_backend` (`virtual_mode=True`); `src/rudra/permissions/floor.py`. Cross-ref **A1.49**, **U.19** |
| A1.49 | **DONE** 2026-08-10 | **The deny floor's path rules do not cover `execute`, and a blocked model routes around them through the shell.** The floor's `outside-root` and `git-dir` rules are evaluated against a resolved path, which only `write_file`/`edit_file`/`delete` carry; `execute` is checked solely against `catastrophic-command` (`rm -rf /`, `mkfs*`, `dd of=/dev/*`). So a shell command may write anywhere the user can. The Step 7 design spec §4.5 states the floor "is not a sandbox and does not pretend to be one", which anticipated a *deliberate* bypass — **this row records the stronger, measured fact: the model finds the bypass on its own, unprompted, immediately after being denied.** Observed in the Step 7 acceptance run (auto mode, task instructed to write outside the project root); the audit log is the whole story: `deny floor write_file /var/.../escaped.txt` → `allow execute pwd` → `deny floor write_file ../tmp.../escaped.txt` → `allow execute echo "hello" > /var/.../escaped.txt`, and the file exists. Two denials, then success by another route, in one run, with no user involvement. **Not fixable by pattern-matching commands** — redirections, `tee`, `cp`, `mv`, `python -c`, and any script the agent writes then runs are all equivalent, which is why real isolation is an OS-level concern (containers, seccomp, a sandbox backend) rather than a regex. **Sharpened by A1.50:** filesystem tools are confined by the backend's `virtual_mode=True`, not by the floor, so `execute` is not merely the weakest path out — it is the *only* one. That narrows the fix surface considerably and makes option (b) below more attractive than it first looks. Options, none taken yet: (a) accept and document prominently, with the audit log as the compensating control — every `execute` is recorded even in auto mode; (b) deny `execute` by default in `auto` mode, so unattended runs get filesystem tools only, which are properly gated; (c) require a `SandboxBackendProtocol` implementation that is genuinely isolated before `--auto` is allowed with shell. **The `ask`-mode default is materially safer and is unaffected** — the user sees the command string before it runs. The exposure is specifically `--auto`/`--yolo` plus shell. Decide before Step 16 ships publicly; the honest interim is (a) plus a blunt warning wherever `--auto` is documented. **RESOLVED 2026-08-10 — owner chose (b), refined: unattended shell is opt-in, not forbidden.** `[tools] shell_in_auto` defaults **false**, so `--auto` alone runs the filesystem tools only — the ones the backend genuinely confines (A1.50). `--allow-shell` or `[tools] shell_in_auto = true` turns commands back on for users who want unattended test runs, knowing the trade. The check sits in `PermissionEngine.decide` **after** the allow rules, deliberately: `allow = ["execute:pytest*"]` names exactly what may run and is therefore itself an opt-in. `ask` mode is untouched — the user reads each command first, so the gap never existed there. **Verified against the original escape scenario:** the same task and model that previously wrote `echo "hello" > /abs/path` after two denials now logs `deny auto-shell execute pwd` and nothing leaves the project; with `--allow-shell` the same command runs and is audited. Downstream cost recorded so Step 8 inherits it rather than rediscovering it: `C3.6`'s test runner and `C6.5`'s fix loop need this flag to run unattended, which is now a deliberate one-line choice instead of a silent default | Step 7 acceptance run, audit log quoted above; `src/rudra/permissions/floor.py` (`_DESTRUCTIVE_PATH_TOOLS` excludes `execute`); Step 7 design spec §4.5. Cross-ref **A1.44**, **§0.8** |

The root is a verified-true match for this machine; the sandbox list is a guess, and is now the fallback for paths that are genuinely *not* under the root. No sandbox behaviour is lost — `/testbed/`, `/workspace/` and friends never match a real root, so they fall straight through to Step 2b. | A1.52 | PENDING | **The agent factories ignore the `project_path` they are given and read config from `Path.cwd()` instead.** `create_coder_agent` calls the process-global `get_config()` (`src/rudra/agent/coder_agent.py:76`), and `create_planner_agent` does the same; `get_config()` calls `build_config()` with no `project_root` (`src/rudra/config/loader.py:314`), whose default is `Path.cwd()` (`:245`). So the project layer (`<project>/.rudra/config.toml`) and the `.env` that feeds the env layer (`:249`) are both read from wherever the process happens to be standing, not from the project being worked on. Invisible in normal use — `rudra` is run from the project root, so cwd and project coincide — and it is the tests that expose it: `build_gate(build_config(tmp_path), tmp_path)` correctly scopes the gate to `tmp_path`, then the factory on the very next line silently reconfigures itself from the repo the test is running inside. **Found 2026-08-11** when `.githooks/pre-push` (A3.7) went red on its first real use: a developer `.env` containing `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=-1` reached four tests that had passed `tmp_path` explicitly. Worked around in the tests for now (A3.8 — `monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)` in `test_agent_wiring.py`'s fixture), which is a workaround and is labelled as one. | A1.57 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | **`run_tests` reports "no tests were collected" as "Tests failed", which would send Step 9's fix loop to repair working code.** `TestResult.passed` is `exit_code == 0` (`src/rudra/testing/runner.py`), and pytest exits **5** when it collects nothing — a distinct code from **1**, which means tests ran and failed. The count parser cannot rescue it either: pytest's "no tests ran in 0.00s" line contains no `N passed`/`N failed` pair, so `parse_counts` correctly returns all-`None` and the summary falls through to the generic failure branch. Measured in Step 8's acceptance run 3 (`--auto --allow-shell`, `gemma4:31b-cloud`): the model called `run_tests` before it had written `test_calc.py`, the audit records `allow execute "python3 -m pytest"`, `.rudra/run/logs/tests.log` ends `collected 0 items` / `no tests ran in 0.00s`, and the transcript shows `run_tests: Tests failed.` — twice. Running the same suite by hand once the test file existed gives `1 passed`. **Why this matters more than the wording:** `C6.6`'s deterministic gate and `C6.5`'s fix loop consume exactly this boolean. A project whose tests have not been written yet, or whose collection is misconfigured, is indistinguishable from one whose tests genuinely fail — so the loop would iterate on correct code, guided by output that names no failing test. It is the same class as `A1.33(b)`: an environment problem reported as a code problem. **Fix:** treat "nothing was collected" as its own outcome rather than a failure, keyed on pytest's exit 5 and on a parsed total of zero, and say so in the summary. **FIXED 2026-08-11.** `TestResult.no_tests_collected` is set by `_collected_nothing`, which keys on two signals because neither covers both cases: pytest's exit 5 is definitive but stack-specific, and a parsed total of zero with a non-zero exit catches runners that report a summary instead. `passed` stays `False` — nothing ran, so nothing is verified — but the summary now says the code was not exercised and that this is neither a pass nor a failure, so the model writes the tests rather than editing working code. Three regression tests assert the three cases stay distinct: empty, genuinely failing, genuinely passing | `src/rudra/testing/runner.py` (`passed = result.exit_code == 0`); `src/rudra/testing/parse.py` (no pair to match in pytest's empty-run line); acceptance run 3 transcript and `tests.log` quoted above. | A1.58 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | **`A1.51`'s new regression test is red on macOS and green on Linux, so the fix it guards is only verified on one platform.** `test_real_project_root_beats_a_sandbox_prefix` (`tests/test_deepagents_path.py:76-86`) parametrizes over four roots — `/app`, `/tmp/work`, `/code/proj`, `/home/user/proj` — and passes each as a literal string while the normalizer compares against a **resolved** root. On macOS two of the four resolve elsewhere: `Path("/tmp/work").resolve()` → `/private/tmp/work` (`/tmp` is a symlink) and `Path("/home/user/proj").resolve()` → `/System/Volumes/Data/home/user/proj` (autofs). The literal path under test therefore no longer sits under the resolved root, the root strip does not fire, and the call falls through to the sandbox-prefix strip — the exact behaviour `A1.51` exists to prevent. `/app` and `/code/proj` have no symlink and pass, which is why it is 2 of 4 rather than all 4. **Measured 2026-08-11 on pristine `origin/main` in a detached worktree, before any Step 8 merge:** `uv run pytest tests/test_deepagents_path.py -q` → `2 failed, 12 passed`, failing on `[/tmp/work]` and `[/home/user/proj]`. So this is **not** merge fallout — `A1.51`'s row claims `520 passed, 2 skipped` on a clean clone, which held on Linux CI and does not hold on this machine. **Two candidate fixes, and they are not equivalent:** (a) resolve the root inside the test, which makes the suite pass but narrows what is being asserted, since a *user* who configures `project_root = /tmp/work` still hits the original bug; or (b) make the normalizer tolerate an unresolved root by comparing both spellings, which is what `PermissionEngine.rule_matches` already does for exactly this reason — macOS resolving `/etc` to `/private/etc` (`src/rudra/permissions/rules.py`). **(b) is the one that matches the defect**, and (a) would paper over it. **FIXED 2026-08-11 with (b), the one that matches the defect.** `_normalize`'s root strip now tries both spellings of the root — as passed and as resolved — against both spellings of the incoming path, and a match on any pair counts. The path's resolved spelling is computed lazily and used *only* for the membership test, never to rewrite what is returned; a path that cannot be resolved (dangling symlink, permission error) simply contributes no second candidate. **Strictly additive by construction:** it can only add matches, so a path genuinely outside the project still falls through untouched — verified live, `/etc/passwd` → `/etc/passwd`. This is the same reasoning, and literally the same `/etc` → `/private/etc` case, that `PermissionEngine.rule_matches` already documents. **Note the defect was real in production, not only in the test:** a user who opens a project at `/tmp/work` gets it recorded as `/private/tmp/work`, and the model writes the spelling it was shown. **Verified against a real `/tmp`-rooted project** (`/tmp/rudra-a158-4dvl`, resolving to `/private/tmp/rudra-a158-4dvl`): the typed spelling and the resolved spelling now both yield `/src/deep/models.py`, where the typed one previously fell through to the sandbox strip. A new test, `test_a_symlinked_root_matches_the_path_the_user_typed`, builds the case from a **real symlink** so it fails on Linux too rather than depending on a macOS quirk — and uses a three-level path deliberately, because Step 2d's last-resort heuristic keeps the final two components and a shallower path passes by accident without the root strip ever running. | A1.66 | **DONE** 2026-08-13 | **Fixed at both levels it broke.** `git_snapshot` now passes `--untracked-files=all`, so git lists untracked *files* instead of collapsing each untracked directory into one entry, and it records a **sha256 fingerprint per path** rather than a bare set of paths — because listing files alone still misses the other half: a file that is untracked before *and* after an attempt appears identically in both listings even when its contents were rewritten, which is exactly what a fix-loop retry does to greenfield code. `changed_since` now counts a path when it is new, gone, or its fingerprint moved. Listing files makes build output visible, so the snapshot prunes it through `verify/stubs.py`'s `SKIP_DIRS`, promoted from private to public so the gate and the ledger cannot hold different opinions about what is build output. Guarded by `tests/test_loop_git_snapshot.py` — 11 cases against **real repositories**, because every one of these is invisible to a mock; 7 of them fail against the old code, including the live shape verbatim. `uv run pytest -q` → `968 passed, 2 skipped`. ~~**`files_touched` records build-output directories, because `git status --porcelain` reports untracked directories as single entries.** Measured 2026-08-12 in Step 9c's first acceptance run: `t1.files_touched` came back as `["__pycache__/", "parser.py", "test_parser.py"]`. `changed_since` (`src/rudra/loop/engine.py`) diffs two `git_snapshot` sets, and porcelain collapses an untracked directory into one `__pycache__/` entry rather than listing its files. **No longer harmless — it blocks real work, measured 2026-08-13 by Step 10a's acceptance run.** `changed_since` computes `after - before`, so when task 1 creates `src/` the snapshot records the single porcelain entry `src/`; task 2 then writes `src/main.rs`, porcelain *still* reports only `src/`, the set difference is **empty**, and the empty-diff guard (`engine.py`, "the coder wrote nothing") blocks a task whose coder wrote a real file. Observed end to end: `t1 done files_touched=['Cargo.toml', 'src/']`, then `t2 blocked 3 attempts []`, `t3 blocked`, `t6 blocked`, `t8 blocked`, all noted "the coder wrote nothing", while `src/main.rs` (45 lines) and `src/lib.rs` (28 lines) existed on disk the whole time. Because the tasks never reached the gate, the fix loop never saw the two compile errors those files contain, and the run exited 1 with `8 requested · 1 done · 4 blocked · 3 dropped`. **This bites every greenfield run after its first task** — the case Rudra is most often pointed at — and the severity below was written before that was measured. Originally recorded as: **Harmless today and wrong anyway:** `verify_project` filters by suffix, so a directory name matches no scanner and the gate is unaffected — but the ledger shows it to the user, and any later consumer that treats `files_touched` as a file list gets a directory. Every stack profile already declares its build-output dirs (`stacks/registry.py`, `ALL_SKIP_DIRS`), which is the filter to apply. Note `verify/stubs.py::source_files` already prunes exactly these for the `--all` path, so the knowledge exists in one place and is simply not consulted here | `src/rudra/loop/engine.py` (`changed_since`, `git_snapshot`); measured ledger entry quoted above; `src/rudra/stacks/registry.py:71` (`ALL_SKIP_DIRS`); `src/rudra/verify/stubs.py` (`source_files` prunes them) |

The user sees `t1    write the parser` with no status — the one thing the tool exists to report. **This is `A1.48`'s class exactly** (`cli.py` rendering `[skills]` as markup and printing nothing), and the remedy is the same: `rich.markup.escape` on tool output before printing, or drop the brackets from `_render`. `A1.48` remains open for the config-error surface; fixing both together is sensible, and whichever is done first should note the other | `src/rudra/loop/tools.py` (`_render` emits `[{status}]`); `src/rudra/agent/planner_agent.py` (`_log_message` prints tool content via `console.print`); cross-ref **A1.48** |
| A1.68 | **DONE** 2026-08-13 | **Both candidate fixes shipped, because they answer different halves.** The row left the choice open; decided against `C6.3`'s intent — the reviewer is advisory, and `git_diff` exists to be *bounded*. **(1) The loop names what it touched:** `review_once` takes the ledger and `_review_prompt` lists every `files_touched` path, deduplicated, blocked tasks included — half-finished work is what most deserves a second opinion. The names come from the ledger rather than a second git call because the loop already has them, and since **A1.66** that record is file-level and correct; this fix would have been built on sand a day earlier. **(2) `git_diff` stops lying:** a tree whose only changes are untracked now returns those filenames instead of `No changes in the working tree`. Naming rather than diffing is deliberate — `git diff --no-index /dev/null <file>` prints every new file in full, which is the uncapped output this tool exists to avoid, and `git add -N` would make the reviewer, which structurally cannot write (S9b.3), mutate the index. Capped at 40 names, build output pruned through the same `SKIP_DIRS` the gate and the loop use. A path-limited or `staged=True` call is left alone: it asked a narrow question. `uv run pytest -q` → `977 passed, 2 skipped`, of which 9 are new and 7 fail against the old code. ~~**The reviewer sees nothing on a greenfield run: `git_diff` shows tracked changes, and new files are untracked.** Measured 2026-08-12 across two Step 9c acceptance runs. The first wrote `parser.py` and `test_parser.py` into a fresh repo and the reviewer reported `No changes in the working tree`; the third, where the coder *edited* a committed `legacy.py`, produced a real finding. So the reviewer works exactly when a file is already tracked and is useless when it is not — which is every greenfield run, the case Rudra is most often pointed at. **The advisory pass is therefore silently absent from the runs that most need it**, and the summary gives no hint, because an empty review prints nothing (`loop/engine.py::review_once` skips on empty text). Candidate fixes, neither obviously right: have `git_diff` include untracked files (changes a Step 8 tool's contract), or hand the reviewer the run's `files_touched` and let it `read_file` them (bounded differently, and it is what the loop already knows). Decide it with `C6.3`'s intent in view — the reviewer is advisory, so the cost of getting this wrong is wasted tokens rather than a wrong verdict | `src/rudra/loop/engine.py` (`review_once`); `src/rudra/tools/git_tools.py` (`git_diff`); the two acceptance runs quoted above |

Twice in one run — `run1.log:117` and `:145` — the planner's reply to a stage prompt was the literal string `<SUBAGENT-STOP>` and nothing else. That tag comes from the vendored bootstrap Step 11b injects into every planner stage: `using-superpowers/SKILL.md:5-7` opens with `<SUBAGENT-STOP>If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, ignore this skill.</SUBAGENT-STOP>`. The model is regurgitating markup from its own system prompt instead of answering. **Harmless in this run** — the loop re-prompted and the second reply was `DONE`, and the run completed with `csvparse.py` and `test_csvparse.py` both written — but it costs a turn each time and is a symptom rather than the disease: prompt markup written for a frontier model is being copied by a small one. **This is `C5.8`'s measurement arriving early, and it is exactly the risk the 11b spec named** (§6, "the plausible failure is over-triggering... written for a frontier model"). Note the same run used **no skills at all**, while the pre-fix attempt of the identical task *did* read `brainstorming` unprompted — so selection is non-deterministic at this size, which is the other half of what `C5.8` must measure. **Do not fix by editing the vendored corpus** (frozen, S11a.3) or by tweaking until one run looks better. The options are: strip `<SUBAGENT-STOP>…</SUBAGENT-STOP>` in `bootstrap_text` the way its YAML frontmatter already is, or replace the verbatim bootstrap with a Rudra-authored primer — the spec isolated that in one function for this reason. Decide in 11c with `C5.8`'s numbers, over several runs, not one | `src/rudra/agent/planner_agent.py` (`bootstrap_text`, which already strips frontmatter and could strip this); `src/rudra/skills/bundles/superpowers/src/skills/using-superpowers/SKILL.md:5-7`; acceptance `run1.log:117,145`; cross-ref **C5.8**, spec `2026-08-17-step11b-skill-wiring-design.md` §6 |
| A1.79 | PENDING | **The path normalizer mangles every backend *route* path, so the model can list a skill but never read one.** Found 2026-08-17 by Step 11b's acceptance run 1 — in code that predates 11b and that 11b's own contract test could not see. `install_path_normalizer`'s Step 2d "last resort" (`compat/deepagents_path.py:208-219`) takes any unmatched absolute path, drops the leading `/`, and if it has more than three segments keeps only the **last two**. `/skills/active/brainstorming/SKILL.md` therefore becomes `/brainstorming/SKILL.md`, which does not exist. Reproduced deterministically: `validate_path("/skills/active/brainstorming/SKILL.md")` → `'/brainstorming/SKILL.md'`; `"/skills/library/superpowers/writing-plans/SKILL.md"` → `'/writing-plans/SKILL.md'`. **The asymmetry is what makes it so confusing in a live run:** `ls("/skills/active/")` normalizes to `/skills/active` (three segments, untouched) and returns the correct listing, so the model sees the skills exist, reads a path straight out of that listing, and is told the file is not found. Acceptance run 1 shows exactly that — `ls` succeeded twice, `read_file` failed three times on paths `ls` had just printed, the model then tried the real host path from its own cache, and the run died at "3 consecutive planner failures" with 0 tasks. **`/artifacts/` escapes only by luck of depth** — `artifacts/large_tool_results/x.md` is three segments, so the `> 3` test is False; one directory deeper and eviction would break the same way, which means this is a latent defect in Step 7's route too, not only in 11b's. **Not caught by `tests/test_skills_backend_contract.py`** because that test exercises the backend directly and never installs the normalizer, which is a monkeypatch applied only during a real run (`main_agent.py:408`). The normalizer exists to rescue *hallucinated* absolute paths; it has no way to know `/skills/` and `/artifacts/` are real mount points. **Fix:** teach it the route prefixes — return the path untouched when it starts with one — and add a test that installs the normalizer *and* reads through the composite, closing the gap between the contract test and the live path | `src/rudra/compat/deepagents_path.py:208-219` (Step 2d); `src/rudra/agent/main_agent.py:408` (`install_path_normalizer`), `:354-363` (the routes it does not know about); acceptance log `run1.log` lines 56-70 |

Not caught because no test compares the prompt a stage is *constructed* with against `build_planner_prompt(stage=...)` — 10b's tests assert on `_tools_for_stage` and on `build_planner_prompt` directly, never on the seam between them. **Fix:** pass `stage=stage`, and add a test asserting each stage's constructed prompt equals its own~~ | `src/rudra/agent/planner_agent.py:115` (the parameter), `:326-332` (the call that omitted it); cross-ref **S10b.1** |

| A3.7 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | **Nothing ran the quality gates before a push, so CI was the *first* thing to run them.** A3.5 and A1.51 both reached `main` because `ruff format --check` and `pytest` were never run locally beforehand — and, worse, would not have been believed if they had been: the working `.venv` had drifted from `uv.lock` and reported `14 failed` for reasons unrelated to any change (missing `langchain_openai`, which `pyproject.toml:47` requires), so genuine failures were indistinguishable from ambient noise. Added `.githooks/pre-push`, which runs `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` and `pytest` and aborts the push on failure. Opt-in per clone via `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` (hooks cannot be shipped pre-enabled — `.git/hooks` is not tracked); `--no-verify` skips it. It prefers `uv run` over `.venv/bin` precisely because of the drift above: `uv run` reconciles against the lockfile first, so a stale venv self-heals instead of producing phantom failures. Falls back to `.venv/bin` with a warning, and exits 1 with instructions if neither is present — a hook that silently passes when its tooling is missing would reproduce the exact defect it exists to prevent. Documented in `Documentation/07-development.md` under "Run the gates before you push" | `.githooks/pre-push`; `git config --get core.hooksPath` → `.githooks`; baseline measurement 2026-08-11: `.venv/bin/pytest -q` on unmodified `main` → `14 failed, 502 passed`, vs `520 passed, 2 skipped` on a clean `uv sync` clone. Cross-ref **A3.5**, **A1.51**, **A3.6** |
| A3.8 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | **The test suite reads the developer's `.env`, so it is red for anyone who actually runs Rudra and green in CI.** `build_config()` calls `load_dotenv(root / ".env")` (`src/rudra/config/loader.py:249`), and `load_dotenv` mutates `os.environ` **process-wide** — so a single test whose root resolves to the real repo imports the developer's `.env` into every test that runs after it, in every file. Measured 2026-08-11 with the `.env` in this working tree: `10 failed, 510 passed` with it present, `520 passed, 2 skipped` with it moved aside — the same tree, the same commit. The trigger was `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=-1`, which is a **legitimate** Ollama value meaning "unlimited" but which the schema rejects: `max_output_tokens in [model.default] must be a positive integer, got -1 (the env layer)`. CI never saw any of it, because `.env` is gitignored and a clean clone has none. This is the noise that made the A3.5/A1.51 breakage invisible locally — the suite was *already* red, so two more failures changed nothing a reader would notice. `test_agent_wiring.py` had carried a local fixture against this class since Step 7, which is evidence the leak was met before and treated as file-local when it is not. **Fixed** with `tests/conftest.py`: an autouse fixture stripping `RUDRA_*`, `OLLAMA_*` and `VERBOSE` before every test, `monkeypatch` restoring them after. That took it 10 → 4. The remaining 4 needed `monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)` in `test_agent_wiring.py`'s fixture, because clearing the variables cannot help when `load_dotenv` re-imports them *during* the test — those tests call factories that consult the cwd-scoped global config (**A1.52**, logged separately as the product-side defect this only papers over). The chdir is deliberately file-local, not in conftest: several other tests read repo files through relative paths and need the real cwd | `src/rudra/config/loader.py:249` (`load_dotenv`), `:245` (`Path.cwd()` default); with `.env` present → `10 failed, 510 passed`, with `.env` moved aside → `520 passed, 2 skipped`; `.env` contains `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=-1`; `tests/test_agent_wiring.py`'s pre-existing local fixture. Verified after the fix **with the `.env` back in place**: `uv run pytest -q` → `520 passed, 2 skipped`. Cross-ref **A1.52**, **A3.7**, **A3.5**, **A1.51** |
| A3.9 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | **CI narrowed to `pull_request` only; `push: branches: [main]` removed.** Owner decision 2026-08-11, after asking twice what CI was actually buying. The honest answer, once **A3.7**'s pre-push hook existed, is: less than it was. Lint, format and the suite all run locally in seconds, and the "3.13 is tested nowhere but CI" argument turned out to be weak — `uv run --python 3.13 pytest -q` fetches the interpreter and runs the suite on it in one command (measured 2026-08-11: `520 passed, 2 skipped` on `Python 3.13.12`). What survives the challenge is narrower and worth stating precisely, because it is the *only* remaining justification: **CI is an environment that is not the committer's.** Every defect found on 2026-08-11 was an environment defect — a `.venv` drifted from `uv.lock` (14 phantom failures) and a gitignored `.env` carrying `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=-1` (10 more, **A3.8**) — and neither can occur on a clean clone. A gate running inside the environment that produced the noise cannot tell you the noise is noise, which is exactly why **A3.5** and **A1.51** reached `main` unnoticed. **The trade is explicit and is the owner's to make:** a direct push to `main` with `--no-verify`, or from a clone where `core.hooksPath` was never set, is now checked by nothing at all — and this repo's normal workflow *is* direct commits to `main`, so in practice CI will rarely run until PRs are used. Revisit at **Phase 10**: external contributors do open PRs, at which point the `pull_request` trigger is the one that matters and this narrowing costs nothing | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`on:` block); 3.13 measurement quoted above; cross-ref **A3.6**, **A3.7**, **A3.8** |

### A5 — Config / environment hygiene (found during Task 8 live smoke run)

| # | Status | Item | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A5.1 | **DONE (load_dotenv half only)** 2026-08-07 | **Local `.env`'s `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=-1` silently overrides `config.py:22`'s documented `131072` default, for *any* process, regardless of invocation directory — and broke the U.12 smoke run against `gemma4:31b-cloud`.** `config.py:9` calls bare `load_dotenv()`, which resolves `.env` relative to the location of the module doing the importing (walking upward from `src/rudra/config.py`'s own directory), not the process's cwd. Task 8's first smoke attempt ran `rudra` from a `mktemp -d` tempdir wholly outside the repo and still picked up the repo's `.env` this way. `.env` sets `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=-1` (a value meaningful to plain local Ollama as "unbounded"), which — because `os.getenv` in `config.py:16-22` only falls back to its coded default when the var is *unset*, not when it's present with an unwanted value — took priority over the `131072` default and was forwarded verbatim into `ChatOllama(num_predict=-1, ...)` (`planner_agent.py:79-85`, `coder_agent.py:61-67`). `langchain-ollama 1.1.0` puts this straight into the request's `options.num_predict` (`langchain_ollama/chat_models.py:782`); Ollama's cloud passthrough for `gemma4:31b-cloud` rejects it server-side: `ollama._types.ResponseError: max_tokens must be positive, got: -1 (status code: 400)`. Reproduced directly: instrumenting `ChatOllama._chat_params` mid-run printed `options={'num_predict': -1, 'temperature': 0.3}` right before the crash. `.env` is gitignored (`git check-ignore -v .env` → `.gitignore:139`), so this is not a tracked-file defect, but the `load_dotenv()` call-site behavior in `config.py:9` is: any contributor with a `.env` anywhere upward of `src/rudra/` — including one copied from `.env.example`, which per **A1.5** already ships a different wrong value (`2048`) — gets a silent override no matter what directory they run `rudra` from. Not fixed here (out of Task 8's scope: no `src/` edits permitted); worked around for U.12 by explicitly exporting `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=131072` on the smoke-run command line, which takes precedence over `.env` per `python-dotenv`'s default `override=False`. Fix belongs with A1.5/A1.15: either `load_dotenv(override=False)` from the actual project root only (not module-relative), or validate `num_predict > 0` in `OllamaConfig` and reject/warn on nonsense values. **Closed 2026-08-07 (Step 3, Task 4) on the first leg only.** `config.py`'s `Config.load()` now calls `load_dotenv(Path.cwd() / ".env")` — an explicit path derived from the invocation directory, not a bare `load_dotenv()` walking upward from this module's own location — with `override=False` retained so a real env var still beats `.env`. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_config.py -v` → `test_dotenv_is_looked_up_at_the_cwd_only`, `test_dotenv_in_the_cwd_is_honored`, and `test_a_real_env_var_beats_dotenv` all pass. **The second leg — validating `num_predict > 0` — is deliberately declined, not merely deferred.** This row's own leg-(1) evidence above already establishes that `-1` is a *valid* value for plain local Ollama, meaning "unbounded," and that only Ollama's **cloud** passthrough rejects it server-side; a validator in `OllamaConfig` would reject a legal value for the local-first default case in order to protect a hosted one it doesn't even talk to by default. Value legality is provider-specific, not a `config.py` concern, and belongs with the Step 5 model factory (**C1.x** — see the note added to **C1.4**), which is the first place in the codebase that will actually know which provider it is talking to. A future session should not read this row's original text and conclude the validation suggestion was silently dropped. **See A5.2** (added 2026-08-07, final review) for a related, still-open gap this fix did not cover: the `.env` lookup is keyed to `Path.cwd()`, not to `--project-dir`'s resolved project root | repro: `python -c` instrumenting `ChatOllama._chat_params`, printed in Task 8 report; `src/rudra/config.py:9,16-22`; `src/rudra/agent/planner_agent.py:79-85`; `.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/langchain_ollama/chat_models.py:782`; `.env:9` (local, gitignored) vs `.env.example` (A1.5). **NOT a 0.7.4 regression — confirmed on all three legs:** (1) `curl` straight at `/api/chat` with `"options":{"num_predict":-1}` and no langchain/deepagents/Rudra anywhere in the path returns `{"StatusCode":400,"error":"max_tokens must be positive, got: -1"}`, while the same request with `131072` succeeds — the constraint is the Ollama **cloud** endpoint's, not the client stack's; `-1` remains valid for plain local Ollama. (2) Constructing `ChatOllama(num_predict=-1, temperature=0.3)` and reading `._chat_params([])['options']` under both `langchain-ollama` versions gives the identical `{'num_predict': -1, 'temperature': 0.3}` — verified in a scratch venv on **1.0.1** (pre-upgrade) and in the repo's own `.venv` on **1.1.0** (current): neither version filters or clamps a non-positive `num_predict`, both forward it verbatim. (3) Therefore this would have failed identically on 0.4.12/1.0.1, so Step 1's "behaves exactly as it does today" success condition is intact **Live acceptance evidence, 2026-08-10 (Step 5).** A model backend finally became reachable (OpenRouter, `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free`, via the new `openai_compatible` provider). A full `rudra "write a python script reverse.py that reverses a string, with an argparse CLI"` ran from a `mktemp -d` outside the repo with **no environment overrides on the command line** — the `.env` in that tempdir was the only configuration. It exited 0, wrote `.rudra/PLAN.md` = `- [x] reverse.py`, generated a 1234-byte `reverse.py`, and the generated script executes correctly: `python reverse.py "hello world"` → `dlrow olleh`. This is the no-override acceptance proof this row had been waiting on since Step 3 — the run needed no `OLLAMA_NUM_PREDICT=131072` export of the kind Steps 1 and 2 both required. |
| A5.2 | **DONE** 2026-08-10 | **Found by final whole-branch review 2026-08-07.** The `.env` lookup fixed by **A5.1** is keyed to the process's current working directory, but the CLI separately accepts `--project-dir`/`-d` to name a different project root — so `rudra -d /path/to/proj "task"`, run from anywhere else, ignores `/path/to/proj/.env` completely and instead reads (or misses) whatever `.env` happens to sit in the invocation directory instead. `Config.load` (`src/rudra/config.py:67`) calls `load_dotenv(Path.cwd() / ".env")` — `Path.cwd()`, not the resolved project path. A5.1's own remediation text asked for `.env` to be read "from the actual project root only"; cwd *is* the project root only when `-d` is absent, a condition A5.1's fix did not check for. Compounding constraint a fixer will hit: `cli.py:212` calls `get_config()` — which caches the `Config` instance for the rest of the process (`config.py`'s module-level `_config`, set in `get_config`) — *before* `project_path` is computed at `cli.py:214` from `project_dir`. A correct fix therefore can't just change the path expression passed to `load_dotenv`; it has to reorder `cli.py` so `project_path` is known before `get_config()` is first called, or make `Config.load` accept an explicit project root instead of reading `Path.cwd()` itself. Should land before the Step 5 model factory (`C1.x`) inherits the same cwd-vs-project-root assumption baked into `Config.load` today | `src/rudra/config.py:67` (`load_dotenv(Path.cwd() / ".env")`); `src/rudra/cli.py:212` (`verbose = get_config().agent.verbose`) vs `:214` (`project_path = get_project_path(project_dir)`) — verified by direct read, `get_config()` precedes `project_path` in source order in the same function body. Cross-ref **A5.1** **DONE 2026-08-10.** Both halves landed and both were required. `Config.load(project_root)` takes the root explicitly instead of reading `Path.cwd()`, and `cli.py` resolves `project_path` **before** its first `get_config()` call — necessary because `get_config()` caches for the process, so no later call can correct which `.env` was read. The Config is seeded unconditionally rather than inside the `if verbose is None` block, which would have skipped it whenever `--verbose`/`--no-verbose` was passed. |
