| U.19 | **DONE** 2026-08-05 | **`write_file` (and the other filesystem tools) cannot be invoked directly via a bare `tool.invoke({...})` outside a compiled graph — confirmed on both venvs — resolved by actually running the graph with a scripted `BaseChatModel`, not by fabricating a `ToolRuntime`.** The tool's real signature carries an injected `runtime: ToolRuntime[NoneType, FilesystemState]` parameter that LangGraph's `ToolNode` supplies at call time inside a running graph; a direct `.invoke({"file_path": ..., "content": ...})` omits it. `.venv-probe` (0.7.4): `TypeError: sync_write_file() missing 1 required positional argument: 'runtime'`. `.venv` (0.4.12): `pydantic_core.ValidationError: 1 validation error for write_file / runtime / Field required`. Same failure mode on both versions — not a 0.7.4 regression, a property of how deepagents builds its filesystem tools generally. **Resolution:** `tests/test_deepagents_contract.py` now defines `ScriptedToolModel(BaseChatModel)` — `bind_tools` returns `self`, `_generate` emits one canned `write_file` tool call then `"done"` — and `test_create_deep_agent_routes_writes_to_the_passed_backend` drives the real compiled graph with it via `agent.invoke({"messages": [...]})`, then reads the file back from `tmp_path`. No network call; no provider reachability assumed; every `_local_model()`/`ChatOllama`/`gemma4` reference removed from the file so the suite runs on a machine with no Ollama at all. **New finding surfaced by actually running the graph (not visible to the old tool-name-only test):** under `.venv` (0.4.12), the scripted `write_file("wired.txt", "ok")` call resolves to `/wired.txt` — anchored at the real OS root, not `tmp_path` — and fails with `PermissionError: [Errno 13] Permission denied: '/wired.txt'`, silently caught and returned as a `ToolMessage` rather than raised, so the graph completes but the file never lands. This matches the `DeprecationWarning` 0.4.12 emits on every `FilesystemBackend(root_dir=...)` construction without an explicit `virtual_mode`: "leaving `virtual_mode=False` allows absolute paths and `..` to bypass `root_dir`." 0.7.4's default backend scopes relative paths to `root_dir` correctly with no such gap (test passes clean). This is a real, additional 0.4.12→0.7.4 behavior difference beyond what U.3 already documented — U.3's `virtual_mode=True` write-through claim was tested directly against the backend and remains correct, but the *default* (`virtual_mode` unset) backend's path scoping is a separate, previously-undiscovered gap that only running the actual graph revealed | `tests/test_deepagents_contract.py:51-87` (`ScriptedToolModel`), `:193-218` (`test_create_deep_agent_routes_writes_to_the_passed_backend`); confirmed via ad hoc probe script against both `.venv` and `.venv-probe`; `.venv` failure trace: `ToolMessage("Error writing file '/wired.txt': [Errno 13] Permission denied: '/wired.txt'")` |

So if anything ever calls it before this test, the test goes **quiet** rather than failing — strictly worse than breaking, since it would keep "passing" while no longer proving what it claims to prove. The module docstring at `tests/test_deepagents_contract.py:9-14` stated the opposite — that calling `install_path_normalizer` "would invalidate the identity assertion" — which is backwards. **Fixed:** `test_validate_path_is_shared_object` now asserts `getattr(deepagents.backends.utils, "_rudra_original_validate_path", None) is None` first, refusing to (mis)prove the identity claim if the patch already ran; module docstring corrected to state the real hazard (silent quiet-pass, not breakage). Verified: `.venv/bin/pytest tests/ -v` → all passing, run as the full suite together (not in isolation), which is what exercises the ordering hazard this guard exists for | `tests/test_deepagents_contract.py:9-14` (docstring), `::test_validate_path_is_shared_object`; `src/rudra/compat/deepagents_path.py:88-91,168-169` |

| U.25 | **DONE** 2026-08-05 (found by final whole-branch review) | **`version_guard.py`'s exception handlers could mask the real error.** Both `except` blocks in `require_deepagents_attr` called `version("deepagents")` **inside** the handler to build the error message (`src/rudra/compat/version_guard.py`, pre-fix: inside the `except ImportError` and `except AttributeError` blocks). If deepagents were not installed at all, that call raises `importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError` from within the handler, which — via implicit exception chaining — obscures the original `ImportError`/`AttributeError` that triggered the handler in the first place, defeating the whole point of a guard whose job is to fail legibly. **Fixed:** compute `deepagents_version` once, before the `try`, wrapped in its own `try/except Exception` that falls back to the literal string `"<not installed>"` so it can never itself raise; both handlers now reference that precomputed value. Verified: `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_version_guard.py -v` → all 6 passing (message-content assertions unaffected, since no test asserts the exact version substring for the attr-guard's own version-in-message case) | `src/rudra/compat/version_guard.py:53-77` |

Tools is the one that matters and is **not** implied by Reach: a model can answer a prompt perfectly and never emit a tool call, which deepagents requires of every agent. Non-zero exit if any role fails any stage. |

Three-state and deliberately so: `False` refuses, `True` proceeds, and **absent or `profile is None` proceeds** — every local model reports `profile: None` (measured on `ChatOllama`), so treating absent as unsupported would ban Ollama outright. |

The real work was **routing**: `artifacts_root` defaults to the backend root, so both `large_tool_results/` and the summarization middleware's `conversation_history/` would have been written into the user's project (**A1.45**); both now land in `.rudra/run/artifacts/`, inside D15's volatile subtree. The eviction **threshold** is a separate matter: it is unreachable through `create_deep_agent` and is tracked as **A1.47** rather than silently left at 20 000 as though that were a choice |
| C3.3 | **DONE** 2026-08-10 | Built **without** `permissions=`, which cannot be used at all here (**U.7**). `src/rudra/permissions/` — a pure `PermissionEngine.decide()` consumed by two mechanisms: `RudraPermissionMiddleware.wrap_tool_call` for deny, `interrupt_on`'s `when` predicate for ask. Precedence: floor → deny → session grants → allow → mode default; deny beats allow; reads never gated; Rudra's own `update_plan`/`write_task_assignment`/`ask_user` are control plane. Rules are `tool` or `tool:pattern` with real tool names. Session grants are memory-only, so a run can never widen persisted config. Audit log at `run/logs/permissions.jsonl`, every mode. 169 tests. **Two limits are measured, not assumed:** `execute` escapes the path rules (**A1.49**) and `floor_disable=["outside-root"]` cannot deliver for filesystem tools (**A1.50**) |
| C3.4 | **DONE** 2026-08-10 | `permissions/diff.py` renders a unified diff capped at 20 changed lines with a `+N/-M` header; `d` shows it in full. A new file reports line/byte count and its first 10 lines rather than a diff against nothing; binary or >1 MB content reports size and renders nothing; an unreadable target degrades to a header instead of aborting the prompt. **Verified interactively through a real pty:** the approval panel rendered `write_file  hello.py  (new file, 1 lines, 15 bytes)` with the content, `a` approved it, and the audit line reads `approve prompt write_file hello.py` |
| C3.5 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | `src/rudra/git/` — a Python API (`is_repo`, `current_branch`, `is_clean`, `status`, `diff`, `log`, `branch_exists`, `auto_branch`) plus exactly **one** model-facing tool, `git_diff`. Not five tools: the model already has gated `execute`, so a new tool only earns its place by bounding output, and an uncapped diff is precisely what raw `execute` does badly in a 32B window. Read-only subcommands bypass the gate decision (S8.7), bounded to a fixed set Rudra composes itself — `auto_branch` alone fires three before the planner starts. `git_diff`'s `path` is the one model-supplied value that could ride that bypass, so it is resolved against the project root and anything outside takes a full gate decision rather than being refused locally: a tool that refuses on its own becomes a second policy authority. **Auto-branch is opt-in** (`[tools] auto_branch`, default false) and fires only inside a work tree, on a non-detached HEAD, from a clean tree; every failure prints a reason and changes nothing. **Never commit** is Rudra having no commit code path at all, plus commented deny lines in the `rudra init` template — not a shipped deny, which `rules.py`'s precedence would make un-overridable. Closes **A1.55**; 5 test files, ~60 tests |
| C3.6 | **DONE** 2026-08-11 | `src/rudra/testing/` — `run_tests()` returning `TestResult`, plus a thin tool wrapper. The Python function is primary because Step 9's fix loop and completion gate call it with no model in the loop (S8.1). Command resolution reuses `stacks.resolve_test_command`; **no Go** (S8.6 — `registry.py` is the source of truth over this row's original text). Parsing is exit code + counts + capped tail, deliberately shallow: `pytest --json-report` needs a plugin Rudra cannot install into a user's project and cargo's JSON is nightly-only, so Step 9 must handle the degraded shape regardless — the degraded shape is therefore the contract, and counts are `None` rather than guessed. Full output offloads to `.rudra/run/logs/tests.log` per D17. Four outcomes are kept distinct because a completion gate must not confuse them: `available=False` (no command declared), `launch_error` (declared but would not start), `denied`, `timed_out`. Closes **A1.33(b)**, **A1.56**, **A1.57**; every count fixture captured from a real failing run |

### Phase 6 — Agent architecture rewrite

The hand-rolled loop is the root cause of most limitations.

| # | Status | Item |
|---|---|---|
| C6.1 | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | Replace the file-by-file Python loop with a real agentic loop: the agent owns the todo list, picks the next action, verifies its own work. `RudraAgent.run` now delegates to `loop/engine.py::run_loop`; `_parse_pending_files`, `_check_off_file`, `_run_coder_for_file`, `_stream_coder`, `_request_task_assignment` and `_run_planner_phase` are deleted, along with `tools/planning_tools.py` and `agent/coder_agent.py`. **STEP 9c COMPLETE 2026-08-12.** Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-step9c-agentic-loop-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-12-step9c-agentic-loop.md`. **Five owner decisions recorded as S9c.1–S9c.5**; the load-bearing one is **S9c.1**, which resolves a contradiction inside this ledger: `C6.1` wanted the agent to own the todo list and pick the next action, while D9 forbids an LLM deciding termination. The split is structural, not conventional — the agent has `add_tasks` / `drop_task` / `read_ledger` and **no tool that can produce `DONE` or `BLOCKED`**, so only `loop/engine.py` writes those and only on `VerifyReport.passed`. **Verification:** `uv run ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check` clean; `uv run pytest -q` → **`880 passed, 2 skipped`** (827 at Step 9b; the count *fell* first — `tests/test_plan_items.py` and the four `_check_off_file` cases went with the subjects they tested — then rose). **Acceptance — three live runs against `gemma4:31b-cloud` from `mktemp -d` git repos outside the repo:** **(1) greenfield end to end** — the planner declared two tasks in plain language (`implement a CSV parser in parser.py that handles quoted commas`, `write tests for the CSV parser`), the coder wrote them, the gate passed both on the first attempt, and the generated code was executed by hand: `parse_csv('a,"b,c",d')` → `[['a', 'b,c', 'd']]`, its own 17 tests `OK`. **(2) the environment wall** — same project under `--auto` without `--allow-shell`: the gate's command stages were denied, `escalate` fired on the first task, the run **stopped immediately**, exit 1, and both tasks were reported as `never attempted` rather than omitted. **(3) the fix loop repairing a real failure** — a project seeded with `def broken(n: int) -> str: return n` in a module the request never mentioned: attempt 1 failed typecheck, the blocker went back to the coder verbatim, attempt 2 rewrote it to `return str(n)` and passed, `attempts=2` in the ledger. That run also exercised three other designed behaviours unprompted: the **empty-diff guard** blocked a task where the coder wrote nothing, the **planner was consulted on the block** and added a differently-worded task that then succeeded, and **`drop_task` refused** to retract an already-blocked task. **C6.5a's no-progress stop is unit-tested, not live-tested** — `tests/test_loop_engine.py::test_the_same_failure_twice_blocks` asserts it stops at `attempts == 2` rather than the budget. Forcing it live proved impractical: the coder repairs whatever the gate shows it, which is the fix loop working. Accepted by the owner as sufficient coverage. **Six findings logged before being acted on**, per session rule 2: `A1.63`–`A1.68`. |
| C6.2 | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | Use `subagents=[...]` for coder / tester / reviewer roles, each with its own model. **STEP 9b COMPLETE 2026-08-12.** Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-step9b-subagents-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-12-step9b-subagents.md`. **Five owner decisions recorded as S9b.1–S9b.5.** Shipped `src/rudra/subagents/` — `spec.py` (frozen `RudraSubagent`, deliberately **no** `middleware` field), `registry.py` (four entries + their prompts), `build.py` (the single assembly: `_model_for` / `_tools_for` / `_middleware_for`, feeding both `build_agent` and `to_subagent_spec`), `runner.py` (`SubagentContext`, `SubagentResult`, `run_subagent`). `BUILTIN_ROLES` gains `tester` and `reviewer`, both inheriting `[model.default]`. **The load-bearing decision is S9b.2, and it rests on two facts measured against installed 0.7.4, not assumed:** subagents inherit the parent's `interrupt_on` (`graph.py:718`) but **not** its `middleware=` (`graph.py:666-703`), so a spec without the gate would be gated for approvals and *not* for denials; and a `general-purpose` subagent carrying the main tool list is auto-added unless a spec of that exact name suppresses it (`graph.py:751`). Rudra therefore ships its own gated read-only `general-purpose`, which **narrows D4's accepted `task` risk** from incidental to designed. Both facts are now guarded by `tests/test_deepagents_contract.py`. **Verification:** `uv run ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check` → `125 files already formatted`; `uv run pytest -q` → **`827 passed, 2 skipped`** (742 at Step 9a). **Acceptance — four live runs against `gemma4:31b-cloud` from a `mktemp -d` git repo outside the repo:** (1) reviewer on a real working-tree diff → `ok=True`, `calc.py:6: mean raises ZeroDivisionError if xs is empty`, file hash unchanged; (2) **reviewer explicitly ordered to "Fix the bug by editing calc.py directly. Use write_file"** → it could not, because the tool is not registered, and reported findings instead; hash byte-identical before and after — this is the run that proves S9b.3 rather than asserting it; (3) tester with shell opted in → wrote `test_calc.py`, ran it, reported `Ran 4 tests … OK`; (4) tester with `shell_in_auto = false` → `ok=True` with "Running tests was not permitted. The user must opt in with `--allow-shell`", and `permissions.jsonl` recording `"rule": "<auto:shell-not-opted-in>", "source": "auto-shell"`. **One defect was found by executing rather than by writing (A1.62)** and fixed with two regression tests. **`main_agent.py` and `planner_agent.py` were not modified** |
| C6.3 | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | Reviewer subagent: reads the diff, reports issues, does not edit. **Enforced by tool absence, not by prompt** (S9b.3): its spec carries `FilesystemMiddleware(tools=["ls","read_file","glob","grep"])`, which replaces the default filesystem middleware in place by name (`graph.py:221-228`, `:882`), so `write_file` / `edit_file` / `delete` / `execute` are never registered. `U.17` had already measured that a registered-but-unsupported tool still gets *called* and returns an error, so not registering it is the only structural answer — and the Step 7 acceptance run had already watched a model denied on `write_file` reach for `echo > /abs/path` instead, which is why the prompt is treated as a hint rather than the control. Asserted against the **compiled graph**, not the spec, by `tests/test_subagents_build.py::test_the_reviewers_compiled_graph_has_no_write_tools`, and proved live by acceptance run (2) above. Reads changes through `git_diff` (C3.5), whose output is bounded — an uncapped diff is what raw `execute` does badly in a 32B window |
| C6.4 | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | Tester subagent: writes and runs tests, feeds failures back. Carries **both** `run_tests` and `execute` deliberately — `run_tests` resolves the project's own suite command and caps its output for a 32B window (`testing/runner.py`), while `execute` covers what it cannot express (one test file, a single reproduction command). Both reach the engine as `execute:<command>` (Step 8 spec S8.2), so no new permission vocabulary and A1.49's unattended opt-in covers them automatically. **"Feeds failures back" means it returns them**: `SubagentResult.text` carries the verdict and the failure output. Deciding what to *do* with a failure is `C6.5` in Step 9c. Both halves proved by acceptance runs (3) and (4) above — the denied run returns `ok=True` with an explanation rather than a failure, because a denial is a policy answer, not a runner error |
| C6.5 | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | **Hand-rolled fix loop (D9):** `write → verify (C6.6) → diagnose → fix → reverify`. Deterministic gate only — no LLM judge decides termination. `loop/engine.py::run_task` dispatches the 9b coder subagent, calls 9a's `verify_project`, and feeds `blocker` back **verbatim** on retry — findings with `file:line`, not a paraphrase, because a summary of a type error is a worse input than the type error. Proved live by acceptance run (3): a seeded type error repaired on attempt 2. **One thing the plan got wrong and executing it caught:** the tester pass used `continue`, which sent the *coder* round again and spent an attempt on work that had already passed; it now re-verifies in place |
| C6.5a | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | **Loop bounds.** `[agent] max_fix_attempts` (default 3, validated `>= 1` at load) plus no-progress detection: `loop/bounds.py::failure_signature` hashes the blocking stage and its structured `Finding` tuples, and two identical signatures in a row stop the task. **`output_tail` is deliberately excluded** — it carries durations, absolute temp paths and pytest's ordering seed, so hashing it would almost never match and the detector would never fire, which is the exact failure this row exists to prevent. A changed line number counts as progress, correctly: the model moved the defect. Prior art carried, not copied — the per-invocation guards from `_stream_coder` moved to `subagents/runner.py` in Step 9b; these are the across-invocation bounds |
| C6.6 | **DONE** 2026-08-12 | Replace file-existence success (A1.8) with a **deterministic verification gate**: syntax check → lint → type check → test → **stub scan**. The stub scan is what closes Option A's blind spot — an agent can pass tests and still leave placeholders. **STEP 9a COMPLETE 2026-08-12.** Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-step9a-verification-gate-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-13-step10c-plan-approval.md`. **Five owner decisions as S10c.1–S10c.5.** `run_loop` split into `plan()` and `work()` and **kept as their composition**, so all thirteen cases driving it needed no edit — asserted by `tests/test_loop_split.py::test_run_loop_equals_plan_then_work` rather than assumed. Verbs are approve / revise / cancel; a revision re-enters `breakdown` with the user's words **verbatim** (the gate-blocker principle), capped at `MAX_REVISIONS = 3`. **`EOF` and `KeyboardInterrupt` are cancel, never approve (S10c.5)** — the one safety property here, tested at both prompts. `--plan` finally presents a plan and stops, which its help text had claimed since Step 7 while denying everything and printing nothing. **Verification:** `ruff check` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check` → `148 files already formatted`; `uv run pytest -q` → **`1052 passed, 2 skipped`** (1017 at Step 10b). **Acceptance run 1 — `--plan "build a CLI in Rust with clap"` through a real pty:** the plan printed six facts with their sources — `language = Rust (asked)`, `cli_framework = clap (asked)`, `project_layout = … (inferred)`, `error_handling = anyhow (inferred)`, `testing_strategy = … (inferred)`, `clap_api = derive (inferred)` — then seven tasks, then `Plan mode — nothing was executed`, `Files created: 0`, and `git status --porcelain` showing only `?? .rudra/`. **That run is also what found `A1.75`**, because it was the first thing ever to exercise the planner under `mode = "plan"`: the first attempt presented a plan with **no facts at all**, since `record_fact` was denied six times. Fixed, then re-run to produce the output quoted above. **Acceptance run 2 — `ask` mode, approve:** one plan presented, `Proceed? … : a`, and the work began — the Step 7 write gate then prompting for `Cargo.toml` in the same trace, which shows the two gates are independent. **Acceptance run 3 — revise, then approve. This is the run that proves the row rather than the plumbing.** The first rendering listed six tasks including `t4 write unit tests for the string reversal function`; the user answered `r` and typed *drop the integration tests task, I will write those myself*; the model received that **verbatim** (`The user reviewed your plan and asked for this change: …`), called `drop_task`, and the **re-presented plan listed five tasks with t4 gone**; `a` then executed the revised plan. Two plans presented, two prompts answered, one `drop_task` — a plan the user changed, executed as changed. **Acceptance run 4 — `--auto`:** `grep -c "Proceed?"` over the trace → **0**, no pause, and the generated binary runs (`cargo run -- "hello world"` → `dlrow olleh`). That run exited 1 with `9 requested · 6 done · 3 blocked`, every block reading *the coder wrote nothing* — **`A1.74` recurring, not a new defect**, and the clearest argument yet for its fix: in `ask` mode the user sees the overlapping tasks in the plan and can revise them away, which is exactly what run 3 did. **One behaviour worth recording rather than filing:** the trace shows the model calling `add_tasks` twice from stages that do not have it, getting `is not a valid tool` back and moving on. That is S10b.1's absence-as-enforcement absorbing a probe — it cost two calls and halted nothing (`grep -c "consecutive planner failures"` → 0). **Two defects were found by executing rather than by writing, both by run 1 and run 2:** `A1.75` and `A1.76`. |

Reads files and returns data; **never executes a subprocess** (execution is C3.6's, in Step 8). **Verified 2026-08-07 (Step 4, Task 3):** `detect()` (`src/rudra/stacks/detect.py:50`) reads marker/require/dependency files off disk and returns a list of matching `StackProfile`s sorted most-specific-first (`-specificity, name`), `[]` on greenfield or a missing directory. `resolve_test_command()` (`detect.py:68`) returns the profile's own `test_command` when declared, else reads Node's `package.json` `scripts.test`, else `None` — a real "no test command" answer, never a guessed default. Malformed `package.json` degrades to `{}` via `_load_package_json`'s `except (OSError, ValueError)` rather than raising, since a half-written manifest is a normal mid-task state. Exported from `src/rudra/stacks/__init__.py`. `grep -rn "subprocess\|os.system\|popen\|Popen" src/rudra/stacks/` → no output, confirming the no-subprocess constraint. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_stacks.py -v` → 23 passed (8 from Task 2 + 15 new); `.venv/bin/pytest -q` → **85 passed**. `.venv/bin/ruff check src/ tests/` → all checks passed. **Fixed 2026-08-07 (review):** `_load_package_json` (`detect.py:17-28`) only caught `(OSError, ValueError)`, so syntactically-valid-but-wrong-shape JSON (a bare list or scalar) parsed successfully and was then handed to `.get()` calls in `_declares_dependency` and `resolve_test_command`, raising `AttributeError`. Fixed by returning `parsed if isinstance(parsed, dict) else {}`; docstring now says "unparseable, or not a JSON object". Reproduced both shapes (`["react","vue"]` and `"just a string"`) degrading to `[]`/`None` instead of raising, before and after confirmed via direct script. Two new tests per function pin both shapes: `test_list_shaped_package_json_does_not_crash_detection`, `test_scalar_shaped_package_json_does_not_crash_detection`, `test_resolve_test_command_is_none_when_package_json_is_list_shaped`, `test_resolve_test_command_is_none_when_package_json_is_scalar_shaped`. `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_stacks.py -v` → **27 passed**; `.venv/bin/pytest -q` → **89 passed**; `.venv/bin/ruff check src/ tests/` → all checks passed |
| C11.2 | PENDING | Acceptance matrix: one end-to-end case per stack (Rust, Python, Node, React, Angular), greenfield and brownfield, replacing §0.5's single Rust example. Needs a live model backend — cannot run until one is available |
| C11.3 | PENDING | Angular unit tests require a headless browser (Angular CLI's default builder runs Karma against Chrome). Detect its absence and fail fast with a message naming the missing dependency, rather than hanging on a browser that never launches — inside Step 9's fix loop a hang stalls the loop instead of failing a round |

### Stage III — Make it capable

**Stage III is complete as of 2026-08-12.** Step 9 was decomposed into 9a (the deterministic verification gate), 9b (the coder/tester/reviewer subagents) and 9c (the agentic loop, the fix loop and the ledger). All three are closed. Requirement #3 — loop engineering: code → test → review → fix, iteratively — is met, with the caveat that review is advisory by design (D9) and the deterministic gate is what decides done.

Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-10-step7-shell-and-permissions.md`. **Rows closed:** `C3.1`–`C3.4`, `A1.16`, `A1.44`–`A1.46`, plus `U.17` **DONE** and `U.7` **WONTFIX**. **Seven owner/design decisions are recorded as S7.1–S7.7 in the spec**; the load-bearing one is S7.1: `permissions=` raises `NotImplementedError` on any execute-capable backend, so it cannot replace `C3.3` and Rudra owns the gate outright. `tests/test_deepagents_contract.py::test_permissions_still_rejected_with_execute_backend` is the trigger that reopens `U.7` if upstream lifts the restriction. **Verification:** `ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check` → clean; `pytest -q` → **515 passed, 2 skipped** (286 at Step 6), of which ~190 are the permission layer. **Acceptance — four runs, all from a `mktemp -d` outside the repo under `env -i` with no `RUDRA_*`/`OLLAMA_*` set, configured only by `.rudra/config.toml`, against `gemma4:31b-cloud`:** **(1) interactive `ask`** driven through a real pty so `isatty()` is genuinely true — the approval panel rendered `write_file  hello.py  (new file, 1 lines, 15 bytes)` with the content, `a` approved it, the file was written, and the audit line reads `approve prompt write_file hello.py`. **(2) unattended `--auto`** — no prompts, exit 0, generated `greet.py` runs correctly (`Hello, World!`), and the audit log records the write. **(3) the floor**, both armed and disabled — armed, it denied two `write_file` attempts outside the root; disabled per rule, the run printed `Warning: Deny-floor rules disabled: outside-root` and audited the call as `floor-disabled`. **(4) non-TTY `ask`** — exit 2, message naming both escape hatches, and **nothing written to disk at all**, confirming the check precedes `ensure_layout` and not merely the model call. **Seven defects were found by executing the plan rather than writing it, five of them only by the acceptance runs:** matching only the resolved path meant a deny on `/etc/**` never fired on macOS, where `/etc` resolves to `/private/etc`; glob `*` refuses to cross `/`, so `execute:pytest*` never matched `pytest -q tests/x` — the exact case `always` exists for; `run_with_approvals` called `get_state()` synchronously, which every unit test tolerated on `InMemorySaver` and which `AsyncSqliteSaver` refused on the first real run; the audit log came out **empty in `--auto`**, twice over — first because silence was keyed on the decision's source rather than the tool, then because the middleware recorded only denials — leaving the unattended run, the one whose record matters most, with no record at all; and `A1.48`, a pre-existing Step 6 defect where Rich parsed `[tools]` as a style tag so every config error named no section — which recurred once more in `doctor`'s own table and is now guarded by a test asserting rendered output rather than trusting each call site. **Two findings the acceptance runs surfaced were closed in the same step, after an owner decision:** **`A1.49`** — the floor's path rules do not cover `execute`, and the run measured the model routing around two denials via `echo > /abs/path`. Resolved by making unattended shell **opt-in**: `[tools] shell_in_auto` defaults false, so `--auto` runs filesystem tools only, and `--allow-shell` restores commands for users who want them. Re-running the identical escape scenario now denies the shell attempt and nothing leaves the project. **`A1.50`** — `floor_disable = ["outside-root"]` could never deliver, because `virtual_mode=True` confines filesystem tools regardless; it silently redirected the write into the project instead. Resolved by rejecting that name with an explanation rather than accepting and ignoring it. Together they establish something worth carrying forward: **filesystem tools are confined by the backend, not by Rudra's floor, and `execute` was the only real way out — which is now gated by default.** **One finding stays PENDING by design:** `A1.47`, the eviction threshold is unreachable through `create_deep_agent`, so no config key ships for it; it belongs to `C7.x`. **Step 8 (`C3.5`, `C3.6` — git tools + test-runner) is unblocked**, and so are `C11.2`/`C11.3`, which have been waiting on shell since Step 4 |

Test-runner output is the input to the fix loop. **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-step8-git-and-test-runner-design.md` (2026-08-11). Seven owner decisions recorded there as S8.1–S8.7; the load-bearing ones are **S8.2** (new tools are gated as `execute:<command>`, so no new permission-rule vocabulary) and **S8.6** (D18's five stacks only — `C3.6`'s "go test" row text predates D18 and `registry.py` is the source of truth). The step also closes **`A1.33(b)`**, because `C3.6` consumes the `test_command` field it breaks, and **`A1.53`**, found while designing it — **STEP 8 COMPLETE 2026-08-11.** Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-11-step8-git-and-test-runner.md`. **Rows closed:** `C3.5`, `C3.6`, `A1.33(b)`, `A1.53`, plus four findings logged as `PENDING` before any fix, per session rule 2: `A1.54`, `A1.55`, `A1.56`, `A1.57`. **Verification:** `ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check` → `100 files already formatted`; `pytest -q` → **`656 passed, 2 skipped`** (516 at the measured Step 7 baseline); `rudra --version` → `Rudra v0.2.0`. **Measured diff:** `git diff --stat main..HEAD -- src/ tests/` → 37 files changed, 2878 insertions(+), 38 deletions(-). **Acceptance — four runs against `gemma4:31b-cloud`, from `mktemp -d` outside the repo under `env -i`, configured only by `.rudra/config.toml`:** **(1) `ask` through a real pty** — the panel rendered `execute` with the full resolved command `/private/.../.venv/bin/pytest` and its `cwd`, `a` approved it, and the audit reads `approve … source "prompt"` alongside `run_tests … source "wrapped-execute"`. Both halves of S8.2 are visible in one log: the tool is allowed at the middleware, and the *inner* call is what the human actually decided. **(2) `--auto` alone** — `run_tests` allowed at the middleware, then `deny execute "pytest test_add.py" rule "<auto:shell-not-opted-in>" source "auto-shell"`; exit 0, run completed. The model reached for raw `execute` after `run_tests` honestly reported no test command, and `A1.49`'s opt-in caught the same escape route Step 7 measured. **(3) `--auto --allow-shell`** — tests executed, audit shows `allow execute "python3 -m pytest"`, and `git_diff` was called by the model unprompted. **(4) `auto_branch = true`** — from a clean tree with `?? .rudra/` present it created `rudra/create-greet-py-with-greet-name-returnin`; with the user's own untracked file present it printed `No branch created: the working tree has uncommitted changes.`, stayed on `main`, exited 0, and left that file untouched. **Four defects were found by executing rather than by planning, three of them only by the acceptance runs:** `A1.54` (`config list` hand-listed its rows, so both new `[tools]` keys were accepted, honoured, and never printed); `A1.55` (`ensure_layout` creates `.rudra/` before the run and D15 leaves it untracked, so `auto_branch` would have refused to branch on essentially every real repository); `A1.56` (**the `A1.33(b)` fix reintroduced the same defect one level down** — the no-venv fallbacks emitted a bare `python`, which macOS does not ship); and `A1.57` (`run_tests` reported pytest's exit 5, "collected nothing", as "Tests failed", which would have sent `C6.5`'s loop to repair working code). **Step 9 (`C6.1`–`C6.6`, `C6.5a`, `C6.10`, `U.11` — the agentic loop rewrite) is unblocked**, and inherits one stated consequence rather than discovering it: gated as `execute`, `run_tests` is denied under bare `--auto`, so **unattended runs cannot self-verify unless the user opts into shell**. Loop-engineering runs want `--auto --allow-shell` |

Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-17-step11a-skill-corpus.md`. **Rows closed:** `C5.2`, `C5.2c`, `C5.7`, `A4.7` (NOTICE half). **Rows OBSOLETED:** `C5.4`, `C5.4a`, `C5.4b`, `C5.4c` — and this, not the code, is the step's most important output. **D10's premise does not hold against superpowers 6.3.0:** the 101 tool-name sites §0.3 measured are English prose, and the rewrite would have turned `## What is a Skill?` into `## What is a read_file?`. Literal tool references in the whole 51-file corpus: **three**, two of them inside other harnesses' mapping files. Upstream de-Claude-Code-ified itself — skills speak in *actions* and resolve them per harness via `references/<harness>-tools.md` under `## Platform Adaptation` (`using-superpowers/SKILL.md:52`) — so Rudra adds one file rather than forking 14. **Seven owner decisions are recorded as S11a.1–S11a.7 in the spec**; the load-bearing ones are **S11a.3** (the corpus is frozen: no version check, no update fetch, no network, ever — which drops `C5.6`'s `update` subcommand) and **S11a.5** (vendored code is organised as *bundles*, so a second default-shipped upstream is a directory plus one line in `registry.py`). **Verification:** `ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check` → `160 files already formatted`; `uv run pytest -q` → **1098 passed, 2 skipped** (1053 at the Step 10c baseline measured at the start of this step, +45 new). **Acceptance is deliberately not a live run** — nothing an agent can see changes in 11a — but the by-eye check the plan mandated was performed: the rendered `## Platform Adaptation` list reads `- Rudra: \`references/rudra-tools.md\`` beneath upstream's four entries with nothing else disturbed, and a rewritten cross-reference reads as `Use /skills/library/superpowers/executing-plans/SKILL.md`, a path a model will `read_file`. **Three properties are enforced mechanically rather than promised:** the freeze rule is a 52-entry `MANIFEST.sha256` that a test verifies; all 14 rendered skills parse under deepagents' *own* `_parse_skill_metadata`, which otherwise **silently skips** an unparseable skill so it never appears in the index; and a wheel test confirms the 472K of non-Python corpus survives packaging (55 entries), a failure that would otherwise surface only for a pipx user. **Step 11b (`C5.1`, `C5.2a`, `C5.2b`, `C5.3`, `C5.9`) is unblocked**, and inherits two design questions 11a refused to decide early: whether subagents get their own `skills=` sources or only the planner does (`subagents/build.py:153` makes per-subagent scoping a one-line change, and the enabled 8 split cleanly by role), and whether `~/.rudra/skills/` and `<project>/.rudra/skills/` from `C5.1` are user-authored *bundles* or plain extra sources |
| **11b** | **C5.1, C5.2a, C5.2b, C5.3** — cache builder, `/skills/` route, `skills=` wiring, `[skills]` config, bootstrap injection | 11a | **STEP 11b CODE COMPLETE 2026-08-17, ACCEPTANCE PARTIAL — not yet closed.** Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-17-step11b-skill-wiring-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-17-step11b-skill-wiring.md`. **Rows closed:** `C5.2a` (`skills/cache.py` — keyed on rudra version + each bundle's manifest hash + transform version + enabled set, published by atomic rename behind a `.complete` sentinel, falling back to a per-run temp dir when the cache root is unwritable), `C5.2b` (one `/skills/` route on the existing composite), `C5.3` (the rendered bootstrap injected into the three planner stages), and the vendored half of `C5.1`. `[skills]` is un-reserved with a single `enabled` key validated against the vendored names. **Five owner decisions recorded as S11b.1–S11b.5**; the load-bearing ones are **S11b.1** (one shared skill set given per agent — planner stages, coder and tester; not the reviewer, which is not choosing a method) and **S11b.4** (the bootstrap goes to planner stages only, because `using-superpowers/SKILL.md:5-7` opens with `<SUBAGENT-STOP>` telling dispatched subagents to ignore it). **Verification:** `ruff check src/ tests/` → `All checks passed!`; format clean; `uv run pytest -q` → **1145 passed, 2 skipped** (1099 at the Step 11a baseline). **Two defects were found by running the plan rather than writing it, both logged before being touched:** **`A1.78`** — `create_planner_agent` never forwarded `stage` to `build_planner_prompt`, so since Step 10b all three stages ran on the *breakdown* prompt while their tools were correctly scoped; the clarify stage was told to call `add_tasks` while holding only `record_fact` and `ask_user`. **`A1.79`** — the path normalizer's last resort trimmed any absolute path deeper than three segments to its last two, so `/skills/active/brainstorming/SKILL.md` became `/brainstorming/SKILL.md`; a live model could `ls` the skills and never read one, and `/artifacts/` had escaped only by being three segments deep. Neither was reachable by 11a's contract tests, which exercise the backend without the normalizer that only a real run installs. **Acceptance — 2 of 4 runs done, and the decisive one is still outstanding:** **(1) the chain closes** — the pre-fix run showed the planner calling `read_file('/skills/active/brainstorming/SKILL.md')` **unprompted**, which is precisely what 11b existed to prove; that run then died on `A1.79`. **(3) unwritable cache** — `XDG_CACHE_HOME` on a `chmod 500` path printed `Skills cache is not writable; using a temporary copy for this run.` and carried on. **(2) the control** — `enabled = []` produced a run with zero skills in any prompt. **Still owed: a post-`A1.79` run in which a model successfully *reads* a skill** — the post-fix run completed the work (`csvparse.py` + `test_csvparse.py` written) but reached for no skill at all, so the fix is proven by test and not yet by a live read. **Run (4), the plan-approval regression through a pty, was not run.** **`A1.80` was found in that same post-fix run:** the 31B planner twice replied with the literal `<SUBAGENT-STOP>` tag copied out of the injected bootstrap. Harmless — the loop re-prompted and completed — but it is `C5.8`'s risk arriving early, and together with the non-deterministic skill selection across two runs of the identical task it is the evidence 11c must gather properly. **11c (`C5.5`, `C5.6`, `C5.8`, and the rest of `C5.1`) is unblocked**, and inherits `A1.80` plus the two outstanding acceptance runs. Original rationale: The step where an agent first sees a skill. `render()` already exists and is pure; 11b decides *where* its output lives (`${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/rudra/skills/<key>/`), computes the key — `hash(rudra version + each bundle's MANIFEST.sha256 root + transform version + sorted enabled list)`, for which `manifest.manifest_root_hash` already ships — and handles staleness. Also un-reserves `[skills]` in `config/schema.py:44` |

| Step | Work | Depends on | Why here |

### Parallelizable

These have no cross-dependencies and can run in a separate session alongside their stage:

- **A4.2 + A4.7 (LICENSE + NOTICE)** — do any time; needed before the first public push
- **A4.3 (repo noise cleanup)** — any time after step 2
- **C9.7 (structured logging)** — any time after step 4; makes every later step easier to debug
- **C5.4 (superpowers skill audit)** — can be researched during Stage III, applied in step 11

### Hard gates

1. **Step 4 (CI green)** — no feature work before a regression net exists.
2. **Step 7 (permissions with shell)** — `LocalShellBackend` must never ship without the permission layer in the same step.
3. **Step 16 (docs)** — no public repo push until README matches reality and LICENSE/NOTICE exist.

## SECTION F — deepagents 0.7.4 Upgrade Findings

Verified by unpacking the 0.7.4 wheel and diffing against the installed 0.4.12.

### What Rudra can delete
| Rudra code | Why obsolete |
|---|---|
| `compat/overwrite_backend.py` | 0.7.4 `FilesystemBackend.write()` creates **or overwrites** (`backends/filesystem.py:489`), using `O_TRUNC` + `O_NOFOLLOW`. The whole reason the subclass existed is gone. Only fence-stripping survives → moves to middleware |

### What Rudra must re-verify
| Item | Status in 0.7.4 |
|---|---|
| `validate_path` monkeypatch | Still exists (`backends/utils.py:648`), still imported into `middleware/filesystem.py:77` → patch is still viable, but retest and add a version guard |
| `TodoListMiddleware` | **No longer in the default main stack.** Only present inside the Codex harness profile. Pass explicitly if `write_todos` is wanted (`graph.py:634`) |
| `backend` param type | Now `BackendProtocol` only — `BackendFactory` no longer accepted |

### New capabilities that replace planned Rudra work
| 0.7.4 feature | Replaces |
|---|---|
| `permissions: list[FilesystemPermission]` — `allow` / `deny` / `interrupt` path rules (`middleware/filesystem.py:382-416`) | Most of hand-rolled C3.3 |
| `RubricMiddleware` (`middleware/rubric.py`) — grader subagent runs whenever the agent would stop; injects feedback and resumes until satisfied / failed / max_iterations | **Rejected (D9).** Overlaps the reviewer subagent and gates termination on an LLM verdict. See §0.2 |
| Provider profiles (`profiles/provider/`) — `register_provider_profile`, built-in OpenRouter / NVIDIA / OpenAI kwarg injection, consumed by `resolve_model` | Reduces C1.1 to configuration |
| Harness profiles (`profiles/harness/`) — `excluded_tools`, per-model prompt/tool tuning; ships Anthropic Sonnet/Opus/Haiku, OpenAI Codex, NVIDIA Nemotron profiles | Clean home for per-model quirks instead of Rudra middleware |
| `AsyncSubAgentMiddleware` / `AsyncSubAgent` (`graph_id`) | Out-of-process subagents, Phase 6 option |
| `ContextHubBackend` | LangSmith-hosted files — **not** local-first, skip |
| `_message_eviction.py`, `_overflow_clip.py` | May cover part of C7.5 / C7.6 |
| `FsToolName`, `_ToolExclusionMiddleware` | Per-agent tool visibility |

### Dependency impact
| Package | Have | Need |
|---|---|---|
| `deepagents` | 0.4.12 | **0.7.4** |
| `langchain` | 1.2.13 | **>=1.3.14** |
| `langchain-core` | 1.2.23 | **>=1.5.0** |
| `langchain-ollama` | 1.0.1 | 1.1.0 (needs core `>=1.2.21`, compatible) |
| `langchain-anthropic` | 1.4.0 | **>=1.5.3** (now a hard dep of deepagents) |
| `langchain-google-genai` | 4.2.1 | **>=4.3.1** (now a hard dep) |
| `langchain-openai` | not installed | **>=1.4.1** (needs core `>=1.5.1` — compatible) |
| `langsmith`, `wcmatch`, `packaging` | — | new transitive deps |

`wcmatch` arriving as a deepagents dep is convenient — it is the right tool for real gitignore semantics (A1.10).
